<div class="q-part"><span class="q-part-kicker">Part 1</span><h2>Learn by running code</h2><p>Everything here runs in Colab, in your browser. Press the button, run the cells, fill in the blanks, see what happens.</p></div>
<section class="q-stage" id="stage-12" data-stage="12"><header class="q-stage-head"><div class="q-stage-title"><span class="q-stage-num">Stage 0<span class="q-badge is-core">Core</span></span><h2>Project 0: from Python basics to your first analysis</h2></div><div class="q-stage-stars" data-stars-for="12" aria-label="Stars earned in this stage"><span class="q-s" data-part="1">&#9733;</span><span class="q-s" data-part="2">&#9733;</span></div></header><p class="q-promise">You will climb from a single value to a full table of data, using DNA sequences as the thing to analyse. By the end you will see how every idea connects to the next. <span class="q-time">About 60 minutes.</span></p><p class="q-colab-row"><a class="q-colab" href="https://colab.research.google.com/drive/{% endraw %}{{ site.data.colab.nb0 }}{% raw %}" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Open in Colab: Notebook 0: Project 0</a><span class="q-colab-note">Free Google account needed. Use File, Save a copy in Drive.</span></p><div class="q-prose"><p>Most introductions teach Python's pieces one at a time and never show how they fit together. Here, the pieces are rungs on one ladder, and each rung is built from the one below:</p><div class="q-table-wrap"><table class="q-table"><thead><tr><th>Rung</th><th>What it is</th><th>Example</th><th>What it gives you</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><strong>value</strong></td><td>One thing: a number, some text, a yes/no.</td><td><code>length = 36</code></td><td>The atoms. Everything else is built from these.</td></tr><tr><td><strong>list</strong></td><td>An ordered pile of values.</td><td><code>lengths = [36, 36, 30, 33]</code></td><td>Loop over it, count it, slice it.</td></tr><tr><td><strong>dictionary</strong></td><td>Labelled values: look things up by name.</td><td><code>{"geneA": 36}</code></td><td>Names attached to data.</td></tr><tr><td><strong>numpy array</strong></td><td>A list built for fast maths on every element at once.</td><td><code>np.array(lengths) / 3</code></td><td>No loops: whole-column arithmetic.</td></tr><tr><td><strong>pandas Series</strong></td><td>An array that also has labels.</td><td><code>pd.Series(lengths, index=names)</code></td><td>Maths <em>and</em> lookup by name.</td></tr><tr><td><strong>pandas DataFrame</strong></td><td>Several Series sharing one set of labels: a table.</td><td><code>pd.DataFrame({"length": ..., "gc": ...})</code></td><td>Filter, group, summarise, plot. Everything in this guide.</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>The Colab notebook for this stage has each rung as a short section with blanks to fill in. The page below explains the thinking, and you can try the same code in the notebook. Everything runs on Colab's computers, so there is nothing to install and nothing to break.</p><h3>Rung 1 and 2: values, lists and dictionaries</h3><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">values</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">gene</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="s2">"geneC"</span>            <span class="c1"># text (a string)</span>
<span class="n">length</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="mi">30</span>                <span class="c1"># a whole number (an int)</span>
<span class="n">is_long</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">length</span> <span class="o">&gt;</span> <span class="mi">32</span>      <span class="c1"># True or False (a bool)</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">gene</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">length</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">is_long</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><p>A <strong>list</strong> holds many values in order. A <strong>dictionary</strong> stores values under names, which is how you answer questions like "what is the length of geneB?" without remembering a position.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">lists and dictionaries</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">names</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"geneA"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"geneB"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"geneC"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"geneD"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="n">lengths</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">36</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">36</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">30</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">33</span><span class="p">]</span>

<span class="n">lengths</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">],</span> <span class="n">lengths</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="o">-</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">],</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">lengths</span><span class="p">)</span>       <span class="c1"># first, last, how many</span>

<span class="n">length_of</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="nb">dict</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="nb">zip</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">names</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">lengths</span><span class="p">))</span>        <span class="c1"># {"geneA": 36, ...}</span>
<span class="n">length_of</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"geneB"</span><span class="p">]</span>

<span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">name</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">names</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">length_of</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">//</span> <span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"codons"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><h3>Rung 3: libraries and numpy</h3><p>Python itself is small on purpose. A <strong>library</strong> is a package of extra tools you load with <code>import</code>. Why bother? Compare computing every length divided by three with a loop versus with <strong>numpy</strong>:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">numpy</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">numpy</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="k">as</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">np</span>

<span class="c1"># the loop way</span>
<span class="n">codons</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[]</span>
<span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">n</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">lengths</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="n">codons</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">append</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">n</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">)</span>

<span class="c1"># the numpy way: the whole list at once</span>
<span class="n">arr</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">np</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">array</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">lengths</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">arr</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="mi">3</span>

<span class="n">arr</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">mean</span><span class="p">(),</span> <span class="n">arr</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">max</span><span class="p">(),</span> <span class="n">arr</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">arr</span> <span class="o">&gt;</span> <span class="mi">31</span><span class="p">]</span>      <span class="c1"># summaries, and a filter</span></code></pre></div><aside class="q-callout is-why"><strong class="q-callout-title">This is the idea behind pandas</strong><p>Doing maths on a whole collection at once is called <strong>vectorising</strong>. It is shorter, easier to read and much faster than a loop. pandas takes the same idea and adds <em>labels</em>.</p></aside><h3>Rung 4 and 5: from arrays to tables</h3><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">pandas</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pandas</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="k">as</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pd</span>

<span class="n">s</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">Series</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">lengths</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">index</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">names</span><span class="p">)</span>       <span class="c1"># an array with labels</span>
<span class="n">s</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"geneB"</span><span class="p">],</span> <span class="n">s</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">mean</span><span class="p">()</span>

<span class="n">table</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">DataFrame</span><span class="p">({</span><span class="s2">"length"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="n">lengths</span><span class="p">},</span> <span class="n">index</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">names</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">table</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"codons"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">table</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"length"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">//</span> <span class="mi">3</span>    <span class="c1"># a new column is just maths on a column</span>
<span class="n">table</span></code></pre></div><p>A DataFrame is a bundle of Series that share the same row labels. Selecting a column gives you a Series back, and everything you did with arrays works on it. That is why <code>df["score"] * 2</code> or <code>df[df["score"] &gt; 1]</code> look so natural in later stages.</p><h3>A taste of Biopython: sequences as objects</h3><p>DNA is text, but treating it as plain text means rewriting reverse-complement and translation yourself. <a href="https://biopython.org/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Biopython</a> is a library with biology-aware tools. Install it (in Colab, once per session) and meet its <code>Seq</code> object, which behaves like a string you already know how to slice:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">Colab cell (Colab may already have it)</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="o">%</span><span class="n">pip</span> <span class="n">install</span> <span class="o">-</span><span class="n">q</span> <span class="n">biopython</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">Seq objects</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">Bio.Seq</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">Seq</span>

<span class="n">dna</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">Seq</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"ATGACCGAATGGCTGTAATGCCGTAACGAT"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">dna</span><span class="p">),</span> <span class="n">dna</span><span class="p">[:</span><span class="mi">6</span><span class="p">]</span>              <span class="c1"># sliced just like a string</span>
<span class="n">dna</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">reverse_complement</span><span class="p">()</span>        <span class="c1"># the other strand, read backwards</span>
<span class="n">dna</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">translate</span><span class="p">()</span>                 <span class="c1"># DNA to protein; * marks a stop</span>
<span class="n">dna</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">translate</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">to_stop</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">True</span><span class="p">)</span>     <span class="c1"># stop reading at the first stop</span></code></pre></div><p>Real sequences live in <strong>FASTA</strong> files: a line starting with <code>&gt;</code> naming the sequence, then its letters. <code>SeqIO.parse</code> reads them. Here the file is a text string so you do not need to upload anything:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">a tiny FASTA file</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">FASTA</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="s2">"""&gt;geneA low-GC test gene</span>
<span class="s2">ATGAAATTTAAAGAAATTATTAAAGAAGTTAAAAAA</span>
<span class="s2">&gt;geneB high-GC test gene</span>
<span class="s2">ATGGGCGCCGGCCGCGCCGGGCCCGGCGCCGCCGGC</span>
<span class="s2">&gt;geneC contains an early stop codon</span>
<span class="s2">ATGACCGAATGGCTGTAATGCCGTAACGAT</span>
<span class="s2">&gt;geneD mixed test gene</span>
<span class="s2">ATGGTGCTGAGCCCGGAAGATAAAACC</span>
<span class="s2">AACGTG</span>
<span class="s2">"""</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">reading FASTA</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">io</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">StringIO</span>
<span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">Bio</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">SeqIO</span>
<span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">Bio.SeqUtils</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">gc_fraction</span>

<span class="n">records</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[]</span>
<span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">rec</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">SeqIO</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">parse</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">StringIO</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">FASTA</span><span class="p">),</span> <span class="s2">"fasta"</span><span class="p">):</span>
    <span class="n">records</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">append</span><span class="p">({</span>
        <span class="s2">"id"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="n">rec</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">id</span><span class="p">,</span>
        <span class="s2">"length"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">rec</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">seq</span><span class="p">),</span>
        <span class="s2">"gc"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="n">gc_fraction</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">rec</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">seq</span><span class="p">),</span>
        <span class="s2">"protein"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="nb">str</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">rec</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">seq</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">translate</span><span class="p">()),</span>
        <span class="s2">"protein_len"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">rec</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">seq</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">translate</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">to_stop</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">True</span><span class="p">)),</span>
    <span class="p">})</span>
<span class="n">records</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">]</span></code></pre></div><h3>Closing the loop: sequences to DataFrame to answer</h3><p>Look at what <code>records</code> is: a <strong>list</strong> of <strong>dictionaries</strong>. That is exactly the shape a DataFrame wants. The ladder just closed: Biopython objects, to dictionaries, to a list, to a table you can filter and plot.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">a table of sequences</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">seqs</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">DataFrame</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">records</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">seqs</span>

<span class="n">seqs</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">seqs</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"gc"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">&gt;</span> <span class="mf">0.5</span><span class="p">]</span>                       <span class="c1"># filter, like Stage 3</span>
<span class="n">seqs</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sort_values</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"gc"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ascending</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">False</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">seqs</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">plot</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">bar</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">x</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"id"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">y</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"gc"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">legend</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">False</span><span class="p">)</span>    <span class="c1"># a first chart</span></code></pre></div><aside class="q-callout is-tip"><strong class="q-callout-title">Reading a whole new library</strong><p>You will not remember every function in numpy, pandas or Biopython, and nobody does. The skill is knowing the ladder: what type of thing do I have, what can that type do, and where do I look it up (the library's documentation, and <code>help(thing)</code>)?</p></aside></div><div class="q-practice"><h3>Practice: your turn</h3><p>Reading code teaches you what code looks like; writing it teaches you to code. Do these in your notebook. Each has a starter, a <strong>check cell</strong> that stays silent when you are right and raises an error when you are not (read it: that is your Stage 2 skill at work), and a hidden solution. Tick <em>Done</em> when the check prints <code>all checks passed</code>.</p><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e12a"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: gc_content from scratch</h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Before using <code>gc_fraction</code>, write the idea yourself. Write <code>gc_content(seq)</code> that takes a string of DNA and returns the <strong>fraction</strong> of its letters that are <code>G</code> or <code>C</code>. Use a loop (or <code>str.count</code>), and add a docstring. Return <code>0</code> for an empty string instead of dividing by zero.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">gc_content</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">seq</span><span class="p">):</span>
    <span class="k">pass</span>   <span class="c1"># TODO</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">gc_content</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"GGCC"</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mf">1.0</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">gc_content</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"ATAT"</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mf">0.0</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">gc_content</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"ATGC"</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mf">0.5</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">gc_content</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">""</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">0</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="nb">abs</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">gc_content</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"ATGACCGAATGGCTGTAATGCCGTAACGAT"</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">-</span> <span class="mi">14</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="mi">30</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">&lt;</span> <span class="mf">1e-9</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">gc_content</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">seq</span><span class="p">):</span>
<span class="w">    </span><span class="sd">"""Fraction of letters in seq that are G or C."""</span>
    <span class="k">if</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">seq</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">:</span>
        <span class="k">return</span> <span class="mi">0</span>
    <span class="n">gc</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="mi">0</span>
    <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">letter</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">seq</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">upper</span><span class="p">():</span>
        <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">letter</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="s2">"GC"</span><span class="p">:</span>
            <span class="n">gc</span> <span class="o">+=</span> <span class="mi">1</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">gc</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">seq</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e12b"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: records to a table, then a question</h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Using the <code>records</code> list from this stage, make a DataFrame <code>seqs</code>, then a second DataFrame <code>rich</code> holding only the genes with GC fraction <strong>above 0.5</strong>, sorted from highest to lowest GC. Finally, in one sentence in a text cell, say which gene is the most GC-rich and why a very GC-rich sequence might be harder to work with in the lab.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">seqs</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="kc">None</span>
<span class="n">rich</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="kc">None</span>   <span class="c1"># TODO</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">assert</span> <span class="nb">list</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">rich</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"id"</span><span class="p">])</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"geneB"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"geneD"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">rich</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"gc"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">is_monotonic_decreasing</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">seqs</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">DataFrame</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">records</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">rich</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">seqs</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">seqs</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"gc"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">&gt;</span> <span class="mf">0.5</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sort_values</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"gc"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ascending</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">False</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div></details></div></div><div class="q-puzzles"><div class="q-puzzle" data-qid="s12p1" data-part="1" data-kind="num" data-value="0.466667" data-dp="3"><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>First star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>Run the code above on the FASTA text. What is the <strong>GC fraction</strong> (share of G and C letters) of <code>geneC</code>, to 3 decimal places?</p></div><div class="q-answer"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s12p1">answer =</label><input id="in-s12p1" type="text" inputmode="decimal" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" autocapitalize="off" placeholder="3 decimal places"><button type="button" class="q-check">Check answer</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p>Look at the <code>gc</code> column of your <code>seqs</code> table, or use <code>gc_fraction</code> on <code>geneC</code>'s sequence.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one way to get it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="nb">round</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">seqs</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">loc</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">seqs</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"id"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"geneC"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"gc"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">iloc</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">],</span> <span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">))</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-puzzle is-part2" data-qid="s12p2" data-part="2" data-kind="int" data-h="7dd0fbf2" hidden><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>Second star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>Translate each gene and stop reading at the first stop codon (<code>to_stop=True</code>). Adding up the protein lengths of all four genes, how many amino acids is that in total?</p></div><div class="q-answer"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s12p2">answer =</label><input id="in-s12p2" type="text" inputmode="decimal" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" autocapitalize="off" placeholder="a whole number"><button type="button" class="q-check">Check answer</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p>The <code>protein_len</code> column already holds each length. Add the column up with <code>.sum()</code>. Notice that <code>geneC</code> is shorter than its DNA suggests, and ask yourself why.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one way to get it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">seqs</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"protein_len"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">())</span></code></pre></div></details></div></div></section>
<section class="q-stage" id="stage-1" data-stage="1"><header class="q-stage-head"><div class="q-stage-title"><span class="q-stage-num">Stage 1<span class="q-badge is-core">Core</span></span><h2>Notebook Ridge: cells, kernels and Python basics</h2></div><div class="q-stage-stars" data-stars-for="1" aria-label="Stars earned in this stage"><span class="q-s" data-part="1">&#9733;</span><span class="q-s" data-part="2">&#9733;</span></div></header><p class="q-promise">You will learn the one rule that explains most notebook weirdness, then use strings, loops and conditions to take a variant name apart. <span class="q-time">About 40 minutes.</span></p><p class="q-colab-row"><a class="q-colab" href="https://colab.research.google.com/drive/{% endraw %}{{ site.data.colab.nb1 }}{% raw %}" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Open in Colab: Notebook 1: Python, kernels and errors</a><span class="q-colab-note">Free Google account needed. Use File, Save a copy in Drive.</span></p><div class="q-prose"><p>A <strong>Jupyter notebook</strong> (<code>.ipynb</code>) mixes code, results and notes in one document. It is made of <strong>cells</strong>. Code cells run Python and show what they produce underneath. Markdown cells hold text, headings and reminders to future you.</p><aside class="q-callout is-tip"><strong class="q-callout-title">Keyboard shortcuts worth learning today</strong><ul><li><kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>Enter</kbd>: run the cell and move to the next one (both Colab and VS Code).</li><li>Colab: <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>/<kbd>Cmd</kbd>+<kbd>M</kbd> then <kbd>A</kbd> or <kbd>B</kbd> inserts a cell above or below; then <kbd>M</kbd> or <kbd>Y</kbd> makes it Markdown or code; then <kbd>D</kbd> deletes it.</li><li>VS Code (and classic Jupyter): <kbd>Esc</kbd> first, then the same letters without <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>M</kbd>.</li></ul></aside><p>Open the notebook with the button above, choose <strong>File, Save a copy in Drive</strong> so your edits are yours, and run it cell by cell. Colab runs Python on a Google computer, so there is nothing to install. The code on this page is also in the notebook, with blanks (<code>____</code>) for you to fill in.</p><h3>The kernel is a running program with a memory</h3><p>Behind the notebook is a <strong>kernel</strong>: a Python process that executes your cells and remembers every variable you have created. The notebook shows the code; the kernel holds the state. They can disagree. Try it:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell 1</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">x</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="mi">10</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell 2</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">x</span> <span class="o">*</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><p>Run both: you get <code>20</code>. Now edit cell 1 to <code>x = 100</code> but <em>do not run it</em>, then re-run cell 2. Still <code>20</code>. The page says 100; the kernel still thinks 10.</p><aside class="q-callout is-why"><strong class="q-callout-title">The notebook rule</strong><p>A notebook is only trustworthy if it works from the top, in order, in a fresh kernel. Get in the habit of <strong>restarting and running everything</strong> before you believe any result, and before you share a notebook. In Colab: <strong>Runtime, Restart session and run all</strong>. In VS Code: the <strong>Restart</strong> and <strong>Run All</strong> toolbar buttons.</p></aside><h3>Printing, and what shows up on its own</h3><p>The last expression in a cell is displayed automatically. <code>print()</code> is for everything else: checking an assumption, logging progress inside a loop, leaving yourself a trail of what happened. F-strings make labels painless:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">n_reads</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="mi">1250</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="sa">f</span><span class="s2">"reads counted: </span><span class="si">{</span><span class="n">n_reads</span><span class="si">}</span><span class="s2">"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="sa">f</span><span class="s2">"as thousands: </span><span class="si">{</span><span class="n">n_reads</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="o">/</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="mi">1000</span><span class="si">:</span><span class="s2">.2f</span><span class="si">}</span><span class="s2">k"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><aside class="q-callout is-note"><strong class="q-callout-title">When to print</strong><p>Print while you are figuring things out; delete or reduce the prints once you trust a step. Loud notebooks hide the one line that matters.</p></aside><h3>Data types, in five lines</h3><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">count</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="mi">24</span>             <span class="c1"># int</span>
<span class="n">score</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="mf">3.49</span>           <span class="c1"># float</span>
<span class="n">name</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="s2">"p.Trp18Ter"</span>    <span class="c1"># str</span>
<span class="n">is_low</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">score</span> <span class="o">&lt;</span> <span class="mi">2</span>     <span class="c1"># bool</span>
<span class="n">variants</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"a"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"b"</span><span class="p">]</span>  <span class="c1"># list</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="nb">type</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">count</span><span class="p">),</span> <span class="nb">type</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">score</span><span class="p">),</span> <span class="nb">type</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="p">),</span> <span class="nb">type</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">is_low</span><span class="p">),</span> <span class="nb">type</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">variants</span><span class="p">))</span></code></pre></div><p><code>type(x)</code> is the first thing to check when something behaves strangely. It is the notebook equivalent of looking at the label on the jar.</p><h3>Strings, and slicing them</h3><p>A <strong>slice</strong> <code>text[start:stop]</code> takes characters from <code>start</code> up to, but not including, <code>stop</code>. Counting starts at 0, and negative numbers count from the end.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">v</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="s2">"p.Trp18Ter"</span>
<span class="c1">#    index: 0123456789...</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">v</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">:</span><span class="mi">5</span><span class="p">])</span>    <span class="c1"># Trp</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">v</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">5</span><span class="p">:</span><span class="o">-</span><span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">])</span>   <span class="c1"># 18</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">v</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="o">-</span><span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">:])</span>    <span class="c1"># Ter</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="nb">int</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">v</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">5</span><span class="p">:</span><span class="o">-</span><span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">])</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">)</span>   <span class="c1"># 19</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">v</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">startswith</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"p."</span><span class="p">),</span> <span class="n">v</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">endswith</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"Ter"</span><span class="p">),</span> <span class="n">v</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">upper</span><span class="p">(),</span> <span class="n">v</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">replace</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"p."</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">""</span><span class="p">))</span></code></pre></div><aside class="q-callout is-trap"><strong class="q-callout-title">Off-by-one</strong><p><code>v[2:5]</code> has three characters, not four, because the stop position is excluded. When a slice is wrong by one, write out the indices on paper. It takes ten seconds.</p></aside><h3>Math, conditions, loops</h3><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">7</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">7</span> <span class="o">-</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">7</span> <span class="o">*</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">7</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">7</span> <span class="o">//</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">7</span> <span class="o">%</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">7</span> <span class="o">**</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">)</span>   <span class="c1"># 9 5 14 3.5 3 1 49</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="nb">round</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mf">3.4902</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">))</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">score</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="mf">1.4</span>
<span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">score</span> <span class="o">&lt;</span> <span class="mf">0.5</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="n">label</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="s2">"low"</span>
<span class="k">elif</span> <span class="n">score</span> <span class="o">&lt;</span> <span class="mf">1.0</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="n">label</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="s2">"in between"</span>
<span class="k">else</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="n">label</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="s2">"wild-type-like"</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">label</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">names</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"WT"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Trp18Ter"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Leu25Pro"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">i</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">name</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="nb">enumerate</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">names</span><span class="p">):</span>
    <span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">i</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">name</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="p">))</span>

<span class="c1"># the same idea in one line, called a list comprehension</span>
<span class="n">lengths</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">name</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">names</span><span class="p">]</span></code></pre></div><p>Python reads close to English: <em>for each name in names, print it</em>. Indentation (four spaces) is how Python knows what is inside the loop or the <code>if</code>, so it is part of the grammar, not decoration.</p><p>Here is the list you will use for the puzzles. Every entry follows the pattern <code>p.</code> + three-letter <em>reference</em> + <em>position</em> + three-letter <em>alternate</em>. (<code>Ter</code> means the protein stops there; the same three letters twice means the amino acid is unchanged, although the DNA underneath was edited.)</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">variants</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"p.Gly12Gly"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Trp18Ter"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Leu25Pro"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Val30Asp"</span><span class="p">,</span>
            <span class="s2">"p.Gln36Ter"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Ile40Asn"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Ser45Thr"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Gly46Arg"</span><span class="p">]</span></code></pre></div></div><div class="q-practice"><h3>Practice: your turn</h3><p>Reading code teaches you what code looks like; writing it teaches you to code. Do these in your notebook. Each has a starter, a <strong>check cell</strong> that stays silent when you are right and raises an error when you are not (read it: that is your Stage 2 skill at work), and a hidden solution. Tick <em>Done</em> when the check prints <code>all checks passed</code>.</p><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e1a"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: count a letter</h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task"><code>seq</code> is a short protein sequence. <strong>Without</strong> using <code>seq.count</code>, use a loop to count how many times the letter <code>"I"</code> appears and store it in <code>n_I</code>.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">seq</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="s2">"MTAIIKEIVSRNKRR"</span>

<span class="n">n_I</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="mi">0</span>
<span class="c1"># TODO: loop over seq and add 1 to n_I for every "I"</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">n_I</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">3</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">seq</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="s2">"MTAIIKEIVSRNKRR"</span>

<span class="n">n_I</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="mi">0</span>
<span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">letter</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">seq</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">letter</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"I"</span><span class="p">:</span>
        <span class="n">n_I</span> <span class="o">+=</span> <span class="mi">1</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e1b"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: letter frequencies</h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Build a <strong>dictionary</strong> <code>counts</code> that maps every letter of <code>seq</code> to how many times it occurs, for example <code>{"M": 1, "I": 3, ...}</code>. Hint: <code>counts.get(letter, 0)</code> returns 0 for a letter you have not seen yet.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">seq</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="s2">"MTAIIKEIVSRNKRR"</span>

<span class="n">counts</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">{}</span>
<span class="c1"># TODO: loop over seq and update counts</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">counts</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"R"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">3</span> <span class="ow">and</span> <span class="n">counts</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"I"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">3</span> <span class="ow">and</span> <span class="n">counts</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"K"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">2</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="nb">sum</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">counts</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">values</span><span class="p">())</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">seq</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">seq</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="s2">"MTAIIKEIVSRNKRR"</span>

<span class="n">counts</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">{}</span>
<span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">letter</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">seq</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="n">counts</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">letter</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">counts</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">get</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">letter</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="mi">1</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e1c"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: filter with a comprehension</h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">From <code>variants</code>, make a list <code>late</code> holding the <strong>positions (as integers)</strong> that are greater than 30, using a single list comprehension.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">variants</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"p.Gly12Gly"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Trp18Ter"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Leu25Pro"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Val30Asp"</span><span class="p">,</span>
            <span class="s2">"p.Gln36Ter"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Ile40Asn"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Ser45Thr"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Gly46Arg"</span><span class="p">]</span>

<span class="n">late</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[]</span>   <span class="c1"># TODO: replace with a list comprehension</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">late</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">36</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">40</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">45</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">46</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">variants</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"p.Gly12Gly"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Trp18Ter"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Leu25Pro"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Val30Asp"</span><span class="p">,</span>
            <span class="s2">"p.Gln36Ter"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Ile40Asn"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Ser45Thr"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Gly46Arg"</span><span class="p">]</span>

<span class="n">late</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="nb">int</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">v</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">5</span><span class="p">:</span><span class="o">-</span><span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">])</span> <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">v</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">variants</span> <span class="k">if</span> <span class="nb">int</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">v</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">5</span><span class="p">:</span><span class="o">-</span><span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">])</span> <span class="o">&gt;</span> <span class="mi">30</span><span class="p">]</span></code></pre></div></details></div></div><div class="q-puzzles"><div class="q-puzzle" data-qid="s1p1" data-part="1"><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>First star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>Using slicing, pull the position number out of every variant, convert it to an integer, and add them all up. What is the sum?</p></div><div class="q-answer"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s1p1">answer =</label><input id="in-s1p1" type="text" inputmode="decimal" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" autocapitalize="off" placeholder="a whole number"><button type="button" class="q-check">Check answer</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p>Every variant has the same shape, so <code>v[5:-3]</code> works for all of them. Loop over the list and add <code>int(...)</code> to a running total starting at 0.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one way to get it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">total</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="mi">0</span>
<span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">v</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">variants</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="n">total</span> <span class="o">+=</span> <span class="nb">int</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">v</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">5</span><span class="p">:</span><span class="o">-</span><span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">])</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">total</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-puzzle is-part2" data-qid="s1p2" data-part="2" hidden><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>Second star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>Classify each variant with a loop and <code>if</code>/<code>elif</code>/<code>else</code>: <strong>synonymous</strong> if the reference and alternate letters are identical, <strong>nonsense</strong> if the alternate is <code>Ter</code>, otherwise <strong>missense</strong>. Report the counts in the order synonymous, nonsense, missense, separated by commas.</p></div><div class="q-answer"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s1p2">answer =</label><input id="in-s1p2" type="text" inputmode="text" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" autocapitalize="off" placeholder="e.g. 3,4,1"><button type="button" class="q-check">Check answer</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p>Reference is <code>v[2:5]</code> and alternate is <code>v[-3:]</code>. Check synonymous first, then nonsense. Keep three counters and print them with an f-string.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one way to get it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">syn</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">non</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">mis</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="mi">0</span>
<span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">v</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">variants</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="n">ref</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">alt</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">v</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">:</span><span class="mi">5</span><span class="p">],</span> <span class="n">v</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="o">-</span><span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">:]</span>
    <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">ref</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="n">alt</span><span class="p">:</span>
        <span class="n">syn</span> <span class="o">+=</span> <span class="mi">1</span>
    <span class="k">elif</span> <span class="n">alt</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"Ter"</span><span class="p">:</span>
        <span class="n">non</span> <span class="o">+=</span> <span class="mi">1</span>
    <span class="k">else</span><span class="p">:</span>
        <span class="n">mis</span> <span class="o">+=</span> <span class="mi">1</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="sa">f</span><span class="s2">"</span><span class="si">{</span><span class="n">syn</span><span class="si">}</span><span class="s2">,</span><span class="si">{</span><span class="n">non</span><span class="si">}</span><span class="s2">,</span><span class="si">{</span><span class="n">mis</span><span class="si">}</span><span class="s2">"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div></details></div></div></section>
<section class="q-stage" id="stage-2" data-stage="2"><header class="q-stage-head"><div class="q-stage-title"><span class="q-stage-num">Stage 2<span class="q-badge is-core">Core</span></span><h2>Traceback Gorge: errors are directions, not insults</h2></div><div class="q-stage-stars" data-stars-for="2" aria-label="Stars earned in this stage"><span class="q-s" data-part="1">&#9733;</span><span class="q-s" data-part="2">&#9733;</span></div></header><p class="q-promise">Every programmer sees red text all day. The skill is not avoiding it, it is reading it fast. You will learn the anatomy of an error and a repeatable way to chase one down. <span class="q-time">About 30 minutes.</span></p><p class="q-colab-row"><a class="q-colab" href="https://colab.research.google.com/drive/{% endraw %}{{ site.data.colab.nb1 }}{% raw %}" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Open in Colab: Notebook 1: Python, kernels and errors</a><span class="q-colab-note">Free Google account needed. Use File, Save a copy in Drive.</span></p><div class="q-prose"><p>An error message is the computer telling you, as precisely as it can, where it got stuck and why. It is more helpful than most feedback you will ever get. Here is a real one from a notebook:</p><div class="q-trace"><pre><code><span class="t-dim">---------------------------------------------------------------------------</span>
<span class="t-err">KeyError</span><span class="t-dim">                                  Traceback (most recent call last)</span>
<span class="t-dim">Cell In[4], </span><span class="t-line">line 2</span>
<span class="t-dim">      1 df = pd.read_csv("data/raw/toy.csv")</span>
<span class="t-arrow">----&gt; 2</span> <span class="t-hot">print(df["Score"].mean())</span>

<span class="t-dim">File ~/miniconda3/envs/pandas-quest/lib/python3.12/site-packages/pandas/core/frame.py:4102, in DataFrame.__getitem__(self, key)</span>
<span class="t-dim">   ... (about thirty lines of pandas internals) ...</span>

<span class="t-err">KeyError: 'Score'</span></code></pre></div><ol><li><strong>Start at the bottom.</strong> <code>KeyError: 'Score'</code> says: you asked for something by name and it is not there. The type tells you the category; the message tells you the specifics.</li><li><strong>Find your line.</strong> The arrow <code>----&gt; 2</code> points at <em>your</em> code. Ignore the middle section about library internals; those are the pandas authors' lines, and the bug is almost never theirs.</li><li><strong>Ask what you would expect.</strong> Which column names really exist? <code>print(df.columns)</code>. You will see <code>score</code>, lowercase. Keys are case-sensitive.</li></ol><h3>The usual suspects</h3><div class="q-table-wrap"><table class="q-table"><thead><tr><th>Error</th><th>Plain-English meaning</th><th>First thing to try</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><code>SyntaxError</code></td><td>Python could not even read the code. A missing colon, quote or bracket. Nothing ran.</td><td>Look at the line it marks and the line <em>above</em>.</td></tr><tr><td><code>NameError</code></td><td>You used a name Python has never seen.</td><td>Typo? Or you did not run the cell that creates it (kernel memory!).</td></tr><tr><td><code>TypeError</code></td><td>Right operation, wrong kind of thing, such as adding a string to a number.</td><td><code>print(type(x))</code> for each thing involved.</td></tr><tr><td><code>ValueError</code></td><td>Right kind of thing, unacceptable value, such as <code>int("abc")</code>.</td><td>Print the value that was passed in.</td></tr><tr><td><code>KeyError</code></td><td>A dictionary key or DataFrame column is not there.</td><td><code>df.columns</code>. Check case and stray spaces.</td></tr><tr><td><code>IndexError</code></td><td>You asked for item 10 of a list with 5 items.</td><td><code>len(...)</code> before indexing.</td></tr><tr><td><code>AttributeError</code></td><td>That object has no such method or attribute.</td><td>Typo, or it is a different type than you think.</td></tr><tr><td><code>FileNotFoundError</code></td><td>The path points to nothing.</td><td>Print the current folder with <code>from pathlib import Path; Path.cwd()</code>.</td></tr><tr><td><code>ModuleNotFoundError</code></td><td>The library is not installed in <em>this</em> environment.</td><td>Colab: install it with <code>%pip install name</code>. On your own computer: wrong kernel or interpreter (Stage 11, step 6).</td></tr></tbody></table></div><h3>A routine that works</h3><ol><li>Read the last line of the traceback out loud, slowly.</li><li>Go to the line the arrow points at. Is it what you expected to run?</li><li>Check what you <em>assumed</em>: <code>print(type(x))</code>, <code>df.shape</code>, <code>df.columns</code>, <code>df.head()</code>.</li><li>Shrink the problem. Reproduce it on three rows, or in a fresh cell with two lines.</li><li>Suspect the kernel. <strong>Restart</strong> and <strong>Run All</strong>. A surprising number of bugs vanish.</li><li>Search the message with your own names removed: <code>KeyError pandas column</code> beats <code>KeyError 'Score'</code>. Read the answer for why it happens, not just a line to copy.</li><li>Still stuck? Write a question someone can answer: what you wanted, what you ran, the full error, what you already tried. That is also the best prompt for an AI assistant, which is a fine tool as long as you understand the fix before pasting it in.</li></ol><aside class="q-callout is-why"><strong class="q-callout-title">Loud failures are a gift</strong><p>The dangerous bugs do not raise errors at all. They give you a plausible, wrong number. This is why analysts check shapes and counts constantly, and use <code>assert</code> to make the computer complain on your behalf:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">assert</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">24</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="sa">f</span><span class="s2">"expected 24 rows, got </span><span class="si">{</span><span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="si">}</span><span class="s2">"</span></code></pre></div></aside><aside class="q-callout is-tip"><strong class="q-callout-title">Errors come one at a time</strong><p>Fixing one error often reveals the next. That is progress, not failure. A script with three bugs will show you three different errors, in the order Python meets them. A <code>SyntaxError</code> always comes first because Python reads the whole file before running a single line.</p></aside><p>Warm-up: four quick questions about reading errors. Then a broken script to fix on your own machine.</p></div><div class="q-practice"><h3>Practice: your turn</h3><p>Reading code teaches you what code looks like; writing it teaches you to code. Do these in your notebook. Each has a starter, a <strong>check cell</strong> that stays silent when you are right and raises an error when you are not (read it: that is your Stage 2 skill at work), and a hidden solution. Tick <em>Done</em> when the check prints <code>all checks passed</code>.</p><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e2a"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: fix three small bugs</h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Each pair of lines has a bug that raises an error. Run the cell, read the error, fix one line at a time. The comment says what you want.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">names</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"WT"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Trp18Ter"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Leu25Pro"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="n">last</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">names</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">]</span>            <span class="c1"># want the last name</span>

<span class="n">scores</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">{</span><span class="s2">"WT"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="mf">3.49</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Trp18Ter"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="mf">1.5</span><span class="p">}</span>
<span class="n">wt_score</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">scores</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"wt"</span><span class="p">]</span>    <span class="c1"># want the WT score</span>

<span class="n">reads</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"120"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"80"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"45"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="n">total</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="nb">sum</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">reads</span><span class="p">)</span>         <span class="c1"># want 245</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">last</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"p.Leu25Pro"</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">wt_score</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mf">3.49</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">total</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">245</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">names</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"WT"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Trp18Ter"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Leu25Pro"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="n">last</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">names</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="o">-</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">]</span>

<span class="n">scores</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">{</span><span class="s2">"WT"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="mf">3.49</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Trp18Ter"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="mf">1.5</span><span class="p">}</span>
<span class="n">wt_score</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">scores</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"WT"</span><span class="p">]</span>

<span class="n">reads</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"120"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"80"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"45"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="n">total</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="nb">sum</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="nb">int</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">r</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">r</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">reads</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e2b"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: catch errors on purpose <span class="q-badge is-stretch">Stretch</span></h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Each snippet below fails in a different way. Loop over them, run each inside <code>try</code>/<code>except Exception as e</code>, and append the <strong>name of the error type</strong> (<code>type(e).__name__</code>) to <code>error_names</code>. You will see all five names from the cheat sheet, and learn how programs survive errors.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">snippets</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span>
    <span class="k">lambda</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="n">undefined_name</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="k">lambda</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="s2">"a"</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="k">lambda</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="p">{</span><span class="s2">"a"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">}[</span><span class="s2">"b"</span><span class="p">],</span>
    <span class="k">lambda</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">][</span><span class="mi">5</span><span class="p">],</span>
    <span class="k">lambda</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="nb">int</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"x"</span><span class="p">),</span>
<span class="p">]</span>

<span class="n">error_names</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[]</span>
<span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">run</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">snippets</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="k">pass</span>   <span class="c1"># TODO: try run(), except Exception as e, record type(e).__name__</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">error_names</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"NameError"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"TypeError"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"KeyError"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"IndexError"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"ValueError"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">snippets</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span>
    <span class="k">lambda</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="n">undefined_name</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="k">lambda</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="s2">"a"</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="k">lambda</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="p">{</span><span class="s2">"a"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">}[</span><span class="s2">"b"</span><span class="p">],</span>
    <span class="k">lambda</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">][</span><span class="mi">5</span><span class="p">],</span>
    <span class="k">lambda</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="nb">int</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"x"</span><span class="p">),</span>
<span class="p">]</span>

<span class="n">error_names</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[]</span>
<span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">run</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">snippets</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="k">try</span><span class="p">:</span>
        <span class="n">run</span><span class="p">()</span>
    <span class="k">except</span> <span class="ne">Exception</span> <span class="k">as</span> <span class="n">e</span><span class="p">:</span>
        <span class="n">error_names</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">append</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="nb">type</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">e</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="vm">__name__</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div></details></div></div><div class="q-puzzles"><div class="q-puzzle" data-qid="s2p1" data-part="1"><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>First star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>Pick the best answer for each.</p></div><div class="q-choices"><fieldset class="q-choice-set"><legend>Which line of a traceback tells you <em>what</em> went wrong?</legend><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s2p1-0" value="a"><span>The first line</span></label><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s2p1-0" value="b"><span>The last line</span></label><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s2p1-0" value="c"><span>The longest line</span></label></fieldset><fieldset class="q-choice-set"><legend><code>KeyError: 'Score'</code> after <code>df["Score"]</code>. The file's header is <code>variant,score,se</code>. What is the fix?</legend><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s2p1-1" value="a"><span>Reinstall pandas</span></label><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s2p1-1" value="b"><span>Use <code>df["score"]</code>. Column names are case-sensitive.</span></label><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s2p1-1" value="c"><span>Restart the computer</span></label></fieldset><fieldset class="q-choice-set"><legend>You uploaded <code>toy.csv</code> in Colab's Files panel (it lands in <code>/content/toy.csv</code>), and <code>pd.read_csv("data/raw/toy.csv")</code> gives <code>FileNotFoundError</code>. Most likely cause?</legend><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s2p1-2" value="a"><span>The CSV is corrupted</span></label><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s2p1-2" value="b"><span>pandas cannot read CSV files</span></label><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s2p1-2" value="c"><span>The path is relative to the current folder, and there is no <code>data/raw/</code> folder there, only <code>toy.csv</code> itself</span></label></fieldset><fieldset class="q-choice-set"><legend><code>total = 0</code>, then <code>for s in ["1.5", "2.5"]: total = total + s</code> gives <code>TypeError: unsupported operand type(s) for +: 'int' and 'str'</code>. What is <code>s</code>?</legend><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s2p1-3" value="a"><span>A string, so int + str fails</span></label><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s2p1-3" value="b"><span>A number</span></label><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s2p1-3" value="c"><span>None</span></label></fieldset></div><div class="q-answer is-choices"><button type="button" class="q-check">Check answers</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p>Question 2: the error names the key it could not find. Compare it, character by character, with the real column name.</p></details></div><div class="q-puzzle is-part2" data-qid="s2p2" data-part="2" hidden><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>Second star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>This code has three separate bugs. Paste it into a new cell and run it, then fix what the errors tell you, one at a time. When it finally runs, it prints a mean score. What is that mean, to 3 decimal places?</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">paste into a cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">scores</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="mf">3.45</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mf">1.55</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mf">2.18</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mf">2.59</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mf">3.82</span><span class="p">]</span>

<span class="n">total</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="mi">0</span>
<span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">s</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">scores</span>
    <span class="n">total</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">total</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="n">s</span>

<span class="n">mean</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">total</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">score</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"mean score: "</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="n">mean</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div></div><div class="q-answer"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s2p2">answer =</label><input id="in-s2p2" type="text" inputmode="decimal" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" autocapitalize="off" placeholder="3 decimal places"><button type="button" class="q-check">Check answer</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p>Expect three different errors, in this order: <code>SyntaxError</code> (a missing character at the end of a line), then <code>NameError</code> (one name is misspelled), then <code>TypeError</code> (you cannot add a string and a float; try an f-string).</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one way to get it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">scores</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="mf">3.45</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mf">1.55</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mf">2.18</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mf">2.59</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mf">3.82</span><span class="p">]</span>

<span class="n">total</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="mi">0</span>
<span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">s</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">scores</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="n">total</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">total</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="n">s</span>
