<span class="n">mean</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">total</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">scores</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="sa">f</span><span class="s2">"mean score: </span><span class="si">{</span><span class="n">mean</span><span class="si">:</span><span class="s2">.3f</span><span class="si">}</span><span class="s2">"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div></details></div></div></section>
<section class="q-stage" id="stage-3" data-stage="3"><header class="q-stage-head"><div class="q-stage-title"><span class="q-stage-num">Stage 3<span class="q-badge is-core">Core</span></span><h2>Table Flats: your first DataFrame</h2></div><div class="q-stage-stars" data-stars-for="3" aria-label="Stars earned in this stage"><span class="q-s" data-part="1">&#9733;</span><span class="q-s" data-part="2">&#9733;</span></div></header><p class="q-promise">Libraries, read a CSV, look around, pick columns and filter rows. The four moves behind most data analysis. <span class="q-time">About 40 minutes.</span></p><p class="q-colab-row"><a class="q-colab" href="https://colab.research.google.com/drive/{% endraw %}{{ site.data.colab.nb2 }}{% raw %}" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Open in Colab: Notebook 2: pandas and functions</a><span class="q-colab-note">Free Google account needed. Use File, Save a copy in Drive.</span></p><div class="q-prose"><p>A <strong>library</strong> is code someone else wrote that you borrow with <code>import</code>. The one that matters most here is <a href="https://pandas.pydata.org/docs/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">pandas</a>, which gives Python a table type called a <strong>DataFrame</strong>: rows are observations, columns are named measurements, and every column has a type.</p><p>Our first table is a tiny made-up experiment. No biology needed: each <em>row</em> is one named item measured once, and four columns (<code>bin1_count</code> to <code>bin4_count</code>) say how many reads fell into each of four buckets, from the lowest signal (bin 1) to the highest (bin 4). </p><p>In the Colab notebook (button above), read the CSV straight from its web address. The file is hosted on this site, so there is nothing to upload:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pandas</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="k">as</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pd</span>

<span class="n">URL</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="s2">"https://atharva-bhagwat.github.io/assets/data/toy_vampseq_bin_counts.csv"</span>
<span class="n">df</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">read_csv</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">URL</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">df</span></code></pre></div><h3>Look before you touch</h3><p>Before any analysis, find out what you actually have. Run each of these in its own cell:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">head</span><span class="p">()</span>                      <span class="c1"># first 5 rows (df.tail() for the last 5)</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">shape</span>                       <span class="c1"># (rows, columns)</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">columns</span>                     <span class="c1"># the column names</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">dtypes</span>                      <span class="c1"># type of each column</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">info</span><span class="p">()</span>                      <span class="c1"># types + missing values in one view</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">describe</span><span class="p">()</span>                  <span class="c1"># count, mean, min, max... of numeric columns</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"variant_type"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">value_counts</span><span class="p">()</span>   <span class="c1"># how many rows per category</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"variant"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">nunique</span><span class="p">()</span>        <span class="c1"># how many different variants</span></code></pre></div><aside class="q-callout is-note"><strong class="q-callout-title">Types you will meet</strong><p>Numbers show as <code>int64</code> or <code>float64</code>. Text is <code>object</code> in pandas 2 and <code>str</code> in pandas 3. Both are fine. If a column that should be numeric shows up as text, something odd is hiding in it, which is worth knowing early.</p></aside><h3>Pick columns</h3><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"variant"</span><span class="p">]</span>                       <span class="c1"># one column -&gt; a Series</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[[</span><span class="s2">"variant"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"replicate"</span><span class="p">]]</span>         <span class="c1"># several columns -&gt; a DataFrame (note the double brackets)</span></code></pre></div><h3>Pick rows by condition</h3><p>A comparison on a column produces a column of <code>True</code>/<code>False</code>. Using that to index the DataFrame keeps only the <code>True</code> rows. This is called a <strong>boolean mask</strong>, and it is the pandas move you will use most.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">is_nonsense</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"variant_type"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"nonsense"</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">is_nonsense</span><span class="p">]</span>

<span class="c1"># combine conditions with &amp; (and), | (or), ~ (not) and wrap each one in parentheses</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"variant_type"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"missense"</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">&amp;</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"replicate"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">)]</span>

<span class="c1"># membership and text tests</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"variant_type"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">isin</span><span class="p">([</span><span class="s2">"nonsense"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"synonymous"</span><span class="p">])]</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"variant"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">str</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">endswith</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"Arg"</span><span class="p">)]</span>

<span class="c1"># the same filter, written as a readable string</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">query</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"variant_type == 'missense' and replicate == 1"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><aside class="q-callout is-trap"><strong class="q-callout-title"><code>and</code> versus <code>&amp;</code></strong><p>Writing <code>df[(a) and (b)]</code> raises <code>ValueError: The truth value of a Series is ambiguous</code>. Python's <code>and</code> wants one yes/no, but you gave it a whole column of them. Use <code>&amp;</code>, and keep the parentheses; without them, the operators bind in an order you did not intend.</p></aside><h3>Select by position or label</h3><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">loc</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">:</span><span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"variant"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"bin1_count"</span><span class="p">]]</span>   <span class="c1"># by label: rows 0 to 2 (inclusive!), named columns</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">iloc</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">:</span><span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">:</span><span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">]</span>                         <span class="c1"># by position: rows 0 and 1 (stop excluded), first two columns</span></code></pre></div><aside class="q-callout is-tip"><strong class="q-callout-title">Read the docs like a map</strong><p>Open <a href="https://pandas.pydata.org/docs/reference/api/pandas.DataFrame.query.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener">the page for <code>DataFrame.query</code></a>. Read the one-line summary, then Parameters, then jump to Examples. You do not have to understand all of it. In a notebook, put the cursor inside a function's brackets and press <kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>Tab</kbd> to see the same text in place.</p></aside></div><div class="q-practice"><h3>Practice: your turn</h3><p>Reading code teaches you what code looks like; writing it teaches you to code. Do these in your notebook. Each has a starter, a <strong>check cell</strong> that stays silent when you are right and raises an error when you are not (read it: that is your Stage 2 skill at work), and a hidden solution. Tick <em>Done</em> when the check prints <code>all checks passed</code>.</p><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e3a"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: name the nonsense</h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Using the toy table <code>df</code>, make a <strong>sorted list of unique variant names</strong> whose <code>variant_type</code> is <code>nonsense</code>, called <code>nonsense_names</code>.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">nonsense_names</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[]</span>   <span class="c1"># TODO: filter df, take the variant column, make it unique and sorted</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">nonsense_names</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"p.Gln36Ter"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Trp18Ter"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Tyr52Ter"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">nonsense_names</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="nb">sorted</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">loc</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"variant_type"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"nonsense"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"variant"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">unique</span><span class="p">())</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e3b"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: which rows are bright?</h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">In <strong>replicate 1</strong>, find the variants whose <code>bin4_count</code> is greater than their <code>bin1_count</code> (more reads in the brightest bin than the dimmest). Store the names as a sorted list called <code>bright</code>. Compare two columns, then combine with the replicate filter.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">bright</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[]</span>   <span class="c1"># TODO</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">assert</span> <span class="s2">"p.Leu25Pro"</span> <span class="ow">not</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">bright</span> <span class="ow">and</span> <span class="s2">"p.Trp18Ter"</span> <span class="ow">not</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">bright</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="s2">"p.Lys50Arg"</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">bright</span> <span class="ow">and</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">bright</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">7</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">rep1</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"replicate"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="n">bright</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="nb">sorted</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">rep1</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">loc</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">rep1</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"bin4_count"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">&gt;</span> <span class="n">rep1</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"bin1_count"</span><span class="p">],</span> <span class="s2">"variant"</span><span class="p">])</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e3c"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: top three</h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Among <code>missense</code> rows of <strong>replicate 2</strong>, sort by <code>bin1_count</code> from largest to smallest and keep the <strong>top 3 variant names</strong> in a list called <code>top3</code>.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">top3</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[]</span>   <span class="c1"># TODO: filter, sort_values(..., ascending=False), head(3), then tolist()</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">top3</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"p.Leu25Pro"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Gly46Arg"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Val30Asp"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">sub</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"variant_type"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"missense"</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">&amp;</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"replicate"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">)]</span>
<span class="n">top3</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">sub</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sort_values</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"bin1_count"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ascending</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">False</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">head</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">)[</span><span class="s2">"variant"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">tolist</span><span class="p">()</span></code></pre></div></details></div></div><div class="q-puzzles"><div class="q-puzzle" data-qid="s3p1" data-part="1"><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>First star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>How many rows are <code>missense</code> <strong>and</strong> from <code>replicate</code> 2?</p></div><div class="q-answer"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s3p1">answer =</label><input id="in-s3p1" type="text" inputmode="decimal" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" autocapitalize="off" placeholder="a whole number"><button type="button" class="q-check">Check answer</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p>Two conditions joined with <code>&amp;</code>, each in parentheses. Then count rows with <code>len(...)</code> or <code>.shape[0]</code>.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one way to get it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">sub</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"variant_type"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"missense"</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">&amp;</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"replicate"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">)]</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">sub</span><span class="p">))</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-puzzle is-part2" data-qid="s3p2" data-part="2" hidden><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>Second star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>Add up every count in the four <code>bin*_count</code> columns across the whole table. What is the grand total of reads?</p></div><div class="q-answer"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s3p2">answer =</label><input id="in-s3p2" type="text" inputmode="decimal" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" autocapitalize="off" placeholder="a whole number"><button type="button" class="q-check">Check answer</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p>Select the four columns as a list, then <code>.sum()</code> sums each column. A second <code>.sum()</code> adds those four numbers together.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one way to get it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">bins</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"bin1_count"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"bin2_count"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"bin3_count"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"bin4_count"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">bins</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">()</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">())</span></code></pre></div></details></div></div></section>
<section class="q-stage" id="stage-4" data-stage="4"><header class="q-stage-head"><div class="q-stage-title"><span class="q-stage-num">Stage 4<span class="q-badge is-core">Core</span></span><h2>Score Falls: new columns, groups and pivots</h2></div><div class="q-stage-stars" data-stars-for="4" aria-label="Stars earned in this stage"><span class="q-s" data-part="1">&#9733;</span><span class="q-s" data-part="2">&#9733;</span></div></header><p class="q-promise">You will turn raw counts into one number per row, summarise it per variant, and line up replicates side by side. <span class="q-time">About 45 minutes.</span></p><p class="q-colab-row"><a class="q-colab" href="https://colab.research.google.com/drive/{% endraw %}{{ site.data.colab.nb2 }}{% raw %}" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Open in Colab: Notebook 2: pandas and functions</a><span class="q-colab-note">Free Google account needed. Use File, Save a copy in Drive.</span></p><div class="q-prose"><p>Raw counts are hard to compare: one row has 20 reads, another has 700. A common fix is a <strong>weighted average</strong> of the bins. Give bin 1 weight 1, bin 2 weight 2, and so on. A row with all its reads in bin 1 scores 1, all in bin 4 scores 4, and anything between lands in between.</p><div class="qcode is-output"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">the formula</span></div><pre><code>score = (1*bin1 + 2*bin2 + 3*bin3 + 4*bin4) / total_reads</code></pre></div><h3>Make new columns without loops</h3><p>pandas works on whole columns at once. That is called <strong>vectorised</strong> code. It is shorter, easier to read, and much faster than looping over rows.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">bins</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"bin1_count"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"bin2_count"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"bin3_count"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"bin4_count"</span><span class="p">]</span>

<span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"total_reads"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">bins</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">axis</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">)</span>                   <span class="c1"># axis=1: add across the columns of each row</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">bins</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">*</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">4</span><span class="p">])</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">axis</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"total_reads"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[[</span><span class="s2">"variant"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"replicate"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"total_reads"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">round</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><aside class="q-callout is-note"><strong class="q-callout-title">What is <code>axis</code>?</strong><p><code>axis=0</code> means "down the rows" (one answer per column). <code>axis=1</code> means "across the columns" (one answer per row). If you get a result with the wrong shape, check the axis first.</p></aside><h3>Sort and rename</h3><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sort_values</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">)</span>                       <span class="c1"># smallest first</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sort_values</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ascending</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">False</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">rename</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">columns</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">{</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="s2">"bin_score"</span><span class="p">})</span></code></pre></div><h3>Summarise by group</h3><p><code>groupby</code> splits the table by a column, applies a summary to each piece, and glues the answers back together: <em>split, apply, combine</em>.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">groupby</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"variant_type"</span><span class="p">)[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">mean</span><span class="p">()</span>

<span class="c1"># several summaries at once, with names you choose</span>
<span class="n">per_variant</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">groupby</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"variant"</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">agg</span><span class="p">(</span>
    <span class="n">mean_score</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"mean"</span><span class="p">),</span>
    <span class="n">reads</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"total_reads"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"sum"</span><span class="p">),</span>
    <span class="n">n_rows</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"size"</span><span class="p">),</span>
<span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">reset_index</span><span class="p">()</span>
<span class="n">per_variant</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sort_values</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"mean_score"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><h3>Pivot: one row per variant, one column per replicate</h3><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">wide</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">pivot</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">index</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"variant"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">columns</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"replicate"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">values</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">wide</span>                       <span class="c1"># columns are named 1 and 2</span>
<span class="n">wide</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">corr</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">wide</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">])</span>      <span class="c1"># how well do the replicates agree?</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">wide</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"diff"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">wide</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">-</span> <span class="n">wide</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">])</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">abs</span><span class="p">()</span>
<span class="n">wide</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sort_values</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"diff"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ascending</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">False</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><aside class="q-callout is-why"><strong class="q-callout-title">A mean can hide the story</strong><p>Two variants can have the same average score for completely different reasons. A quick look at the raw bins shows it:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell (no star for this one)</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">pick</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"replicate"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">&amp;</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"variant"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">isin</span><span class="p">([</span><span class="s2">"p.Ile40Asn"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"p.Gly46Arg"</span><span class="p">])]</span>
<span class="n">frac</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">pick</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">bins</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">div</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">pick</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"total_reads"</span><span class="p">],</span> <span class="n">axis</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">)</span>   <span class="c1"># each row now sums to 1</span>
<span class="n">frac</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">index</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">pick</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"variant"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="n">frac</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">T</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">plot</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">bar</span><span class="p">()</span>                                      <span class="c1"># bins on x, one colour per variant</span></code></pre></div><p><code>p.Ile40Asn</code> piles up in the middle bins. <code>p.Gly46Arg</code> splits between the lowest and highest. Same average, different shapes. Stage 8 is all about plots that expose this.</p></aside><aside class="q-callout is-tip"><strong class="q-callout-title">Look at your pivot table before trusting it</strong><p>If one variant's replicates disagree wildly, ask why <em>before</em> averaging them. The answer is sometimes in a column you already have.</p></aside></div><div class="q-practice"><h3>Practice: your turn</h3><p>Reading code teaches you what code looks like; writing it teaches you to code. Do these in your notebook. Each has a starter, a <strong>check cell</strong> that stays silent when you are right and raises an error when you are not (read it: that is your Stage 2 skill at work), and a hidden solution. Tick <em>Done</em> when the check prints <code>all checks passed</code>.</p><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e4a"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: a fraction column</h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Continue in your Stage 4 notebook, where <code>df</code> already has <code>total_reads</code>. Add a column <code>frac_bin4</code>: the share of each row's reads that fall in bin 4. Then find the <strong>replicate 1</strong> variant with the highest <code>frac_bin4</code> and store its name in <code>top_variant</code>.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">top_variant</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="s2">""</span>   <span class="c1"># TODO</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">assert</span> <span class="s2">"frac_bin4"</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">columns</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"frac_bin4"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">between</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">all</span><span class="p">()</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">top_variant</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"p.Lys50Arg"</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"frac_bin4"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"bin4_count"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"total_reads"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="n">rep1</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"replicate"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="n">top_variant</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">rep1</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">loc</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">rep1</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"frac_bin4"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">idxmax</span><span class="p">(),</span> <span class="s2">"variant"</span><span class="p">]</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e4b"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: scores by type</h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Make a Series <code>type_means</code> with the mean <code>score</code> for each <code>variant_type</code>. Two of its facts will be checked: which type scores lowest, and which highest.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">type_means</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="kc">None</span>   <span class="c1"># TODO: groupby + mean</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">type_means</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">idxmin</span><span class="p">()</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"nonsense"</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">type_means</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">idxmax</span><span class="p">()</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"wild_type"</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">type_means</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">4</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">type_means</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">groupby</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"variant_type"</span><span class="p">)[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">mean</span><span class="p">()</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e4c"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: a summary table <span class="q-badge is-stretch">Stretch</span></h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Build a DataFrame <code>summary</code> with <strong>one row per variant</strong> (variant name as the index) and two columns: <code>mean_score</code> (mean over replicates) and <code>rep_diff</code> (absolute difference between replicate 1 and 2). Combine <code>groupby</code>, <code>pivot</code> and a join, or use <code>agg</code> cleverly. Several routes work.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">summary</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="kc">None</span>   <span class="c1"># TODO</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">summary</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">shape</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">12</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="nb">list</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">summary</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">columns</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"mean_score"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"rep_diff"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="nb">abs</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">summary</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">loc</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"p.Ala58Val"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"rep_diff"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">-</span> <span class="mf">1.15</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">&lt;</span> <span class="mf">1e-9</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">wide</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">pivot</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">index</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"variant"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">columns</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"replicate"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">values</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">summary</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">DataFrame</span><span class="p">({</span>
    <span class="s2">"mean_score"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="n">wide</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">mean</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">axis</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">),</span>
    <span class="s2">"rep_diff"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">wide</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">-</span> <span class="n">wide</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">])</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">abs</span><span class="p">(),</span>
<span class="p">})</span></code></pre></div></details></div></div><div class="q-puzzles"><div class="q-puzzle" data-qid="s4p1" data-part="1"><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>First star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>What is the mean <code>score</code> of the <code>WT</code> rows (both replicates), to 2 decimal places?</p></div><div class="q-answer"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s4p1">answer =</label><input id="in-s4p1" type="text" inputmode="decimal" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" autocapitalize="off" placeholder="2 decimal places"><button type="button" class="q-check">Check answer</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p>Filter to rows where <code>variant == "WT"</code> and take <code>.mean()</code> of the <code>score</code> column.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one way to get it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"variant"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"WT"</span><span class="p">][</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">mean</span><span class="p">()</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">round</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">))</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-puzzle is-part2" data-qid="s4p2" data-part="2" hidden><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>Second star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>Pivot the scores so replicates sit side by side, and take the absolute difference between replicate 1 and 2 for each variant. Which variant has the <strong>largest</strong> disagreement? Write its name exactly as it appears.</p></div><div class="q-answer"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s4p2">answer =</label><input id="in-s4p2" type="text" inputmode="text" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" autocapitalize="off" placeholder="p.Xxx00Yyy"><button type="button" class="q-check">Check answer</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p>Use the <code>pivot</code> and <code>diff</code> code above, then <code>idxmax()</code> on the diff column, or sort descending and read the top row. Then look at that variant's <code>total_reads</code> and ask whether you trust it.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one way to get it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">wide</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">pivot</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">index</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"variant"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">columns</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"replicate"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">values</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">wide</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"diff"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">wide</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">-</span> <span class="n">wide</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">])</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">abs</span><span class="p">()</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">wide</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"diff"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">idxmax</span><span class="p">())</span></code></pre></div></details></div></div></section>
<section class="q-stage" id="stage-5" data-stage="5"><header class="q-stage-head"><div class="q-stage-title"><span class="q-stage-num">Stage 5<span class="q-badge is-core">Core</span></span><h2>Function Forge: write it once, reuse it forever</h2></div><div class="q-stage-stars" data-stars-for="5" aria-label="Stars earned in this stage"><span class="q-s" data-part="1">&#9733;</span><span class="q-s" data-part="2">&#9733;</span></div></header><p class="q-promise">You will turn working notebook code into a documented function, learn the habits that make it safe to reuse, and test it. <span class="q-time">About 40 minutes.</span></p><p class="q-colab-row"><a class="q-colab" href="https://colab.research.google.com/drive/{% endraw %}{{ site.data.colab.nb2 }}{% raw %}" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Open in Colab: Notebook 2: pandas and functions</a><span class="q-colab-note">Free Google account needed. Use File, Save a copy in Drive.</span></p><div class="q-prose"><p>In Stage 4 you computed scores for the whole table. Now imagine a collaborator wants a different weighting, or you want to ignore rows with too few reads, or you need the same calculation for next month's dataset. Copy and paste works exactly once. The second time you paste, the two copies start to differ, and you forget which one you trust.</p><aside class="q-callout is-why"><strong class="q-callout-title">The rule of two</strong><p>The second time you copy a block of code, turn it into a function instead. A function is a named, reusable recipe: you fix a bug in one place, and every use of it gets the fix.</p></aside><h3>Anatomy of a function</h3><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">BINS</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"bin1_count"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"bin2_count"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"bin3_count"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"bin4_count"</span><span class="p">]</span>


<span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">add_scores</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">weights</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">4</span><span class="p">),</span> <span class="n">min_reads</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">):</span>
<span class="w">    </span><span class="sd">"""Add total_reads and a weighted-average bin score.</span>

<span class="sd">    score = sum(weight_i * count_i) / total_reads, so 1 means every read</span>
<span class="sd">    is in bin 1 (lowest signal) and 4 means every read is in bin 4.</span>

<span class="sd">    Rows with fewer than `min_reads` total reads get score = NaN, because</span>
<span class="sd">    a number built from 20 reads is not one we should trust.</span>

<span class="sd">    Returns a new DataFrame; the input is left unchanged.</span>
<span class="sd">    """</span>
    <span class="n">out</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">copy</span><span class="p">()</span>
    <span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"total_reads"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">BINS</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">axis</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">BINS</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">*</span> <span class="nb">list</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">weights</span><span class="p">))</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">axis</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"total_reads"</span><span class="p">]</span>
    <span class="n">out</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">loc</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"total_reads"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">&lt;</span> <span class="n">min_reads</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="nb">float</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"nan"</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">out</span></code></pre></div><ul><li><code>def name(parameters):</code> starts the recipe. Parameters with <code>=</code> have <strong>defaults</strong>, so callers only mention what they want to change.</li><li>The triple-quoted text right under <code>def</code> is the <strong>docstring</strong>. It is what <code>help(add_scores)</code> and <kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>Tab</kbd> show.</li><li><code>return</code> hands the result back. A function with no <code>return</code> gives back <code>None</code>, a classic cause of "why is my variable empty?".</li><li><code>df.copy()</code> means the function works on its own copy and never changes the caller's table behind their back.</li></ul><h3>Use it</h3><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">scored</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">add_scores</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">)</span>                    <span class="c1"># defaults</span>
<span class="n">strict</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">add_scores</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">min_reads</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">50</span><span class="p">)</span>      <span class="c1"># same recipe, one dial changed</span>
<span class="n">flat</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">add_scores</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">weights</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">))</span>

<span class="n">scored</span><span class="p">[[</span><span class="s2">"variant"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"replicate"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"total_reads"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">head</span><span class="p">()</span></code></pre></div><h3>Check it, so you can stop worrying about it</h3><p>An <code>assert</code> silently passes when true and shouts when false. A few of them under a function are tiny, permanent tests:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">assert</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">scored</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">)</span>                          <span class="c1"># no rows lost</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">scored</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">between</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">4</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">all</span><span class="p">()</span>              <span class="c1"># scores live in 1-4</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">strict</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">isna</span><span class="p">()</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">()</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">2</span>                <span class="c1"># exactly the 2 low-read rows were blanked</span>

<span class="n">before</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">columns</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">tolist</span><span class="p">()</span>
<span class="n">add_scores</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">columns</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">tolist</span><span class="p">()</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="n">before</span>                     <span class="c1"># the input was not modified</span></code></pre></div><aside class="q-callout is-note"><strong class="q-callout-title">If an assert fails</strong><p>You will get an <code>AssertionError</code> pointing at that line. That is the point. It is a loud failure that says "your assumption was wrong", which is far better than a silent wrong answer. Print the pieces and look.</p></aside><h3>Documentation is a kindness to future you</h3><ul><li><strong>Name things for what they are.</strong> <code>add_scores</code> beats <code>func2</code>. Verbs for actions, nouns for data.</li><li><strong>Docstrings say what and why;</strong> comments explain the <em>why</em> of a non-obvious line, not what the code already says.</li><li><strong>Markdown cells are your lab notebook.</strong> Before each section write one sentence: what question this answers and what you decided.</li><li><strong>Keep functions small and single-purpose.</strong> If the docstring needs the word "and" three times, split it.</li><li><strong>Show an example</strong> in the docstring or the next cell. Examples are the documentation people actually read.</li></ul><p>Later, in Part 2, you will move this function out of the notebook into its own file so that every notebook and script can import it.</p></div><div class="q-practice"><h3>Practice: your turn</h3><p>Reading code teaches you what code looks like; writing it teaches you to code. Do these in your notebook. Each has a starter, a <strong>check cell</strong> that stays silent when you are right and raises an error when you are not (read it: that is your Stage 2 skill at work), and a hidden solution. Tick <em>Done</em> when the check prints <code>all checks passed</code>.</p><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e5a"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: fraction_in_bin</h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Write a function <code>fraction_in_bin(df, k)</code> that returns a Series: the fraction of each row's reads that fall in bin <code>k</code> (1 to 4). Include a docstring, and <code>raise ValueError("k must be 1, 2, 3 or 4")</code> if <code>k</code> is anything else.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">fraction_in_bin</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">k</span><span class="p">):</span>
    <span class="c1"># TODO: docstring, validation, calculation</span>
    <span class="k">pass</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">f</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">fraction_in_bin</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">4</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="nb">abs</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">f</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">iloc</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">-</span> <span class="mi">376</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="mi">694</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">&lt;</span> <span class="mf">1e-9</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">f</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">between</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">all</span><span class="p">()</span> <span class="ow">and</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">f</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">try</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="n">fraction_in_bin</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">5</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="k">raise</span> <span class="ne">AssertionError</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"k=5 should raise ValueError"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">except</span> <span class="ne">ValueError</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="k">pass</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">fraction_in_bin</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="vm">__doc__</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">fraction_in_bin</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">k</span><span class="p">):</span>
<span class="w">    </span><span class="sd">"""Fraction of each row's reads in bin k (1 to 4)."""</span>
    <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">k</span> <span class="ow">not</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">4</span><span class="p">):</span>
        <span class="k">raise</span> <span class="ne">ValueError</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"k must be 1, 2, 3 or 4"</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="n">bins</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"bin1_count"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"bin2_count"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"bin3_count"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"bin4_count"</span><span class="p">]</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="sa">f</span><span class="s2">"bin</span><span class="si">{</span><span class="n">k</span><span class="si">}</span><span class="s2">_count"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">bins</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">axis</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e5b"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: flag_low_coverage</h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Write <code>flag_low_coverage(df, min_reads=50)</code> returning a <strong>boolean Series</strong> that is <code>True</code> for rows whose total reads (sum of the four bins) are below <code>min_reads</code>.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">flag_low_coverage</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">min_reads</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">50</span><span class="p">):</span>
    <span class="k">pass</span>   <span class="c1"># TODO</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">flag_low_coverage</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">()</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">2</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">flag_low_coverage</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">min_reads</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">10_000</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">all</span><span class="p">()</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="ow">not</span> <span class="n">flag_low_coverage</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">min_reads</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">any</span><span class="p">()</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">flag_low_coverage</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">min_reads</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">50</span><span class="p">):</span>
<span class="w">    </span><span class="sd">"""True for rows with fewer than min_reads total reads."""</span>
    <span class="n">bins</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"bin1_count"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"bin2_count"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"bin3_count"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"bin4_count"</span><span class="p">]</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">bins</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">axis</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">&lt;</span> <span class="n">min_reads</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e5c"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: summarise_variants <span class="q-badge is-stretch">Stretch</span></h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Reuse <code>add_scores</code> from this stage. Write <code>summarise_variants(df, min_reads=50)</code> returning a DataFrame indexed by variant with two columns: <code>mean_score</code> (mean over replicates, ignoring masked rows) and <code>n_used</code> (how many rows contributed). Calling a function from inside another is how real code grows.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">summarise_variants</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">min_reads</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">50</span><span class="p">):</span>
    <span class="k">pass</span>   <span class="c1"># TODO: scored = add_scores(...), then groupby("variant")</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">s</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">summarise_variants</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">s</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">shape</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">12</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">s</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">loc</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"p.Ala58Val"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"n_used"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">0</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">s</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">loc</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"WT"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"n_used"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">2</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="nb">abs</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">s</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">loc</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"WT"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"mean_score"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">-</span> <span class="mf">3.49</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">&lt;</span> <span class="mf">0.01</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">summarise_variants</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">min_reads</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">50</span><span class="p">):</span>
<span class="w">    </span><span class="sd">"""Mean score and number of usable rows per variant."""</span>
    <span class="n">scored</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">add_scores</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">min_reads</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">min_reads</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">scored</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">groupby</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"variant"</span><span class="p">)[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">agg</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">mean_score</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"mean"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">n_used</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"count"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div></details></div></div><div class="q-puzzles"><div class="q-puzzle" data-qid="s5p1" data-part="1"><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>First star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>Define <code>add_scores</code> in your notebook and call it on the toy table. What is the <code>score</code> of <code>p.Ser45Thr</code> in <strong>replicate 1</strong>, to 3 decimal places?</p></div><div class="q-answer"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s5p1">answer =</label><input id="in-s5p1" type="text" inputmode="decimal" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" autocapitalize="off" placeholder="3 decimal places"><button type="button" class="q-check">Check answer</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p>Call <code>add_scores(df)</code>, then filter on both <code>variant</code> and <code>replicate</code>, and pick the <code>score</code> column.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one way to get it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">scored</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">add_scores</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">row</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">scored</span><span class="p">[(</span><span class="n">scored</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"variant"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"p.Ser45Thr"</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">&amp;</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">scored</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"replicate"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">)]</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="nb">round</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">row</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">iloc</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">],</span> <span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">))</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-puzzle is-part2" data-qid="s5p2" data-part="2" hidden><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>Second star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>Reuse the same function with <code>min_reads=50</code>. Among rows with <code>variant_type</code> equal to <code>missense</code>, what is the mean <code>score</code> now (3 decimal places)? Rows with too few reads should not count. pandas skips <code>NaN</code> when it averages.</p></div><div class="q-answer"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s5p2">answer =</label><input id="in-s5p2" type="text" inputmode="decimal" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" autocapitalize="off" placeholder="3 decimal places"><button type="button" class="q-check">Check answer</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p>One call to <code>add_scores(df, min_reads=50)</code>, filter to missense, <code>.mean()</code>. If your answer feels too high or too low, check how many rows went into it with <code>.count()</code>.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one way to get it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">strict</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">add_scores</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">min_reads</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">50</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">mis</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">strict</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">strict</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"variant_type"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"missense"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="nb">round</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">mis</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">mean</span><span class="p">(),</span> <span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">),</span> <span class="n">mis</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">count</span><span class="p">())</span></code></pre></div></details></div></div></section>
<section class="q-stage" id="stage-7" data-stage="7"><header class="q-stage-head"><div class="q-stage-title"><span class="q-stage-num">Stage 6<span class="q-badge is-core">Core</span></span><h2>Audit Pass: trust, but verify</h2></div><div class="q-stage-stars" data-stars-for="7" aria-label="Stars earned in this stage"><span class="q-s" data-part="1">&#9733;</span><span class="q-s" data-part="2">&#9733;</span><span class="q-s" data-part="3">&#9733;</span></div></header><p class="q-promise">You have been handed a table with thousands of rows. Before you plot anything you will learn what it measures, find out what is in it and what is missing, and check that the labels mean what they say. <span class="q-time">About 45 minutes.</span></p><p class="q-colab-row"><a class="q-colab" href="https://colab.research.google.com/drive/{% endraw %}{{ site.data.colab.nb3 }}{% raw %}" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Open in Colab: Notebook 3: the PTEN data</a><span class="q-colab-note">Free Google account needed. Use File, Save a copy in Drive.</span></p><div class="q-prose"><p>Open notebook 3 with the Colab button above. Real data arrives with quirks, so the habit that separates careful analysts from unlucky ones is a <strong>data audit</strong>: a short, boring, systematic look at the table before any conclusions.</p><h3>What this dataset is about</h3><p><strong>PTEN</strong> is a human protein that acts as a brake on cell growth signalling. It is an enzyme (a phosphatase) that removes a phosphate group from a signalling lipid, which dampens a pathway that tells cells to grow. When PTEN is damaged, the brake weakens, and changes in the PTEN gene are linked to several cancers and to inherited conditions. Geneticists keep finding single-letter changes in the gene. Many swap one amino acid in the protein for another (a <strong>missense</strong> variant), and for most of them nobody knows whether the change matters.</p><p>A <strong>multiplexed assay of variant effect</strong> attacks that gap by making thousands of variants at once and measuring each one. This table comes from an experiment that measured <strong>abundance</strong>: how much of each PTEN variant ends up in the cell. Each variant is tagged with a fluorescent marker and put into cells. Cells are then sorted into four bins from dim to bright and sequenced, so each variant gets a count of reads per bin. That is exactly the shape of the toy table from Stage 3, only with about four thousand variants. A change that makes the protein unstable gets cleared away by the cell, so its cells look dim and its reads pile up in the low bins.</p><aside class="q-callout is-why"><strong class="q-callout-title">Abundance is not activity, and activity is not disease</strong><p>A variant can be abundant but inactive, or scarce and still partly working. Abundance data tells you which changes destabilise the protein, which is one useful ingredient when judging whether a variant is harmful.
