It does not tell you the final answer.</p></aside><h3>Two scores, one idea</h3><p>Be careful: the toy table and this table both have a column called <code>score</code>, but they are <strong>not the same measurement</strong>.</p><div class="q-table-wrap"><table class="q-table"><thead><tr><th></th><th>Toy table (Stage 4)</th><th>PTEN table (this stage)</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>How it was made</td><td>You computed it: a weighted average of the four bins, weights 1 to 4.</td><td>Published by the researchers, from their own analysis of the sorted-cell data.</td></tr><tr><td>Typical range</td><td>1 to 4. Higher means brighter cells.</td><td>Roughly 0 to 1 (a few outside). It has been rescaled.</td></tr><tr><td>Anchors</td><td>None. 4 means all reads in the brightest bin.</td><td>The reference protein is set to 1. Broken, cut-short proteins sit near 0.</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>They share an idea: summarise <em>where along the dim-to-bright spectrum a variant's cells land</em> as one number. But the procedures and scales differ, so never compare raw numbers across the two tables. Compare patterns <em>within</em> a table, measured against that table's own reference points. You will check this yourself in a moment.</p><h3>Load it</h3><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pandas</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="k">as</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pd</span>

<span class="n">BASE</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="s2">"https://atharva-bhagwat.github.io/assets/data/"</span>
<span class="n">df</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">read_csv</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">BASE</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="s2">"pten-variant-abundance.csv"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">head</span><span class="p">()</span></code></pre></div><p>What the columns hold, in plain terms (<code>score</code> is the one that matters most):</p><div class="q-table-wrap"><table class="q-table"><thead><tr><th>Column</th><th>Meaning</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><code>hgvs_pro</code></td><td>Variant name such as <code>p.Asn12Ile</code>: reference amino acid, position, new amino acid. This is the identity of a row.</td></tr><tr><td><code>score</code></td><td>The measured abundance, already normalised: about 1 means "like the unchanged reference", about 0 means "like a broken protein".</td></tr><tr><td><code>sd</code>, <code>se</code>, <code>lower_ci</code>, <code>upper_ci</code></td><td>Spread and uncertainty of that measurement.</td></tr><tr><td><code>expts</code></td><td>How many experiments contributed to the score.</td></tr><tr><td><code>record_type</code></td><td><code>missense</code> (one amino acid swapped), <code>synonymous</code> (the DNA changed but the amino acid did not), <code>nonsense</code> (a stop signal, so the protein is cut short), <code>wild_type</code> (the reference itself).</td></tr><tr><td><code>abundance_class</code>, <code>abundance_class_label</code></td><td>A binned version of the score, as a number and as a text label.</td></tr><tr><td><code>accession</code>, <code>snv</code></td><td>Database identifier, and a flag for whether one DNA change can produce the variant.</td></tr></tbody></table></div><aside class="q-callout is-note"><strong class="q-callout-title">Why look at synonymous and nonsense variants first?</strong><p>They are your reference points. Nonsense variants cut the protein short, so they should look broken (scores near 0). Synonymous variants keep the amino acid sequence, so they are expected to look like the reference (near 1). "Expected" is the important word: a silent DNA change can occasionally matter for other reasons, which is why you check rather than assume.</p></aside><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell: do the anchors look right?</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">groupby</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"record_type"</span><span class="p">)[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">median</span><span class="p">()</span></code></pre></div><h3>The audit checklist</h3><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">shape</span>                                    <span class="c1"># rows, columns</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">dtypes</span>                                   <span class="c1"># anything numeric stored as text?</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">isna</span><span class="p">()</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">()</span>                             <span class="c1"># missing values per column</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"hgvs_pro"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">is_unique</span>                    <span class="c1"># one row per variant?</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">duplicated</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">subset</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"hgvs_pro"</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">()</span>      <span class="c1"># how many repeats</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"record_type"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">value_counts</span><span class="p">()</span>            <span class="c1"># counts per type</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"abundance_class_label"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">value_counts</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">dropna</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">False</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">crosstab</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"abundance_class"</span><span class="p">],</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"abundance_class_label"</span><span class="p">])</span>   <span class="c1"># do number and label agree?</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"sd"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"se"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"expts"</span><span class="p">]]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">describe</span><span class="p">()</span></code></pre></div><aside class="q-callout is-tip"><strong class="q-callout-title">Compare with what you were told</strong><p>If a collaborator tells you how many rows and how many of each record type to expect, check that your table agrees. If a number differs, that is not a nuisance, it is a finding. Write down why.</p></aside><h3>Turn names into columns, one small step at a time</h3><p>A variant name packs three facts into one string. To summarise by position later, you need them as separate columns. You already know the first tool: slicing.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">name</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="s2">"p.Asn12Ile"</span>
<span class="n">name</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">:</span><span class="mi">5</span><span class="p">],</span> <span class="n">name</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="o">-</span><span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">:]</span>      <span class="c1"># reference and new amino acid</span></code></pre></div><p>The same slicing works on a whole column through <code>.str</code>:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"ref"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"hgvs_pro"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">str</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">:</span><span class="mi">5</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"alt"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"hgvs_pro"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">str</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="o">-</span><span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">:]</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[[</span><span class="s2">"hgvs_pro"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"ref"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"alt"</span><span class="p">]]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sample</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">5</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">random_state</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><aside class="q-callout is-trap"><strong class="q-callout-title">A method that works on most rows is the dangerous kind</strong><p>Look at a synonymous name such as <code>p.Asn12=</code>. The last three characters are <code>12=</code>, not an amino acid. Check the odd ones on purpose:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"hgvs_pro"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">str</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">endswith</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"="</span><span class="p">)][[</span><span class="s2">"hgvs_pro"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"alt"</span><span class="p">]]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">head</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div></aside><p>Fix them with a filter you already know, and then pull out the position, which is the digits in the middle. The <code>\d+</code> below is a tiny <strong>regular expression</strong>, a pattern language for text: <code>\d</code> means a digit and <code>+</code> means one or more.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">loc</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"hgvs_pro"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">str</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">endswith</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"="</span><span class="p">),</span> <span class="s2">"alt"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="s2">"="</span>

<span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"position"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">to_numeric</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"hgvs_pro"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">str</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">extract</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="sa">r</span><span class="s2">"(\d+)"</span><span class="p">)[</span><span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">])</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">astype</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"Int64"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"position"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">isna</span><span class="p">()]</span>       <span class="c1"># which rows have no position, and is that expected?</span></code></pre></div><p>The <code>Int64</code> type (capital I) is a whole number that can also be missing. The rows without a position, such as the wild-type reference row, are your cue to look at them, not ignore them.</p><p>Last, test that the labels agree with the contents. If they do not, you have found a problem worth knowing about now:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">assert</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">loc</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"record_type"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"nonsense"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"alt"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"Ter"</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">all</span><span class="p">()</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">loc</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"record_type"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"synonymous"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"alt"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"="</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">all</span><span class="p">()</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">loc</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"record_type"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"missense"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"alt"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">notna</span><span class="p">()</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">all</span><span class="p">()</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"labels and names agree"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><h3>Package the steps as a function</h3><p>You will use this parsing in every later notebook, so wrap the steps in a function (in Part 2 it moves into a file you can import). This version uses one longer pattern with named parts that does the three extractions at once. It is optional reading: paste it into <a href="https://regex101.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">regex101.com</a> with a few variant names to see each piece light up.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">function: parse_hgvs</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">parse_hgvs</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">column</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"hgvs_pro"</span><span class="p">):</span>
<span class="w">    </span><span class="sd">"""Split names like 'p.Asn12Ile' into ref, position and alt columns.</span>

<span class="sd">    ref = 'Asn', position = 12 (nullable integer), alt = 'Ile'.</span>
<span class="sd">    Synonymous names like 'p.Asn12=' get alt = '='. Anything that does not</span>
<span class="sd">    match (for example the wild-type row '_wt') gets missing values.</span>
<span class="sd">    """</span>
    <span class="n">pattern</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="sa">r</span><span class="s2">"^p\.(?P&lt;ref&gt;[A-Za-z]</span><span class="si">{3}</span><span class="s2">)(?P&lt;position&gt;\d+)(?P&lt;alt&gt;[A-Za-z]</span><span class="si">{3}</span><span class="s2">|=)$"</span>
    <span class="n">parts</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">column</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">str</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">extract</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">pattern</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="n">out</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">copy</span><span class="p">()</span>
    <span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"ref"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">parts</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"ref"</span><span class="p">]</span>
    <span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"position"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">to_numeric</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">parts</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"position"</span><span class="p">])</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">astype</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"Int64"</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"alt"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">parts</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"alt"</span><span class="p">]</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">out</span></code></pre></div><p>Then in a notebook: <code>df = parse_hgvs(df)</code>. The notebooks for the next stages include this function in their first cell.</p></div><div class="q-practice"><h3>Practice: your turn</h3><p>Reading code teaches you what code looks like; writing it teaches you to code. Do these in your notebook. Each has a starter, a <strong>check cell</strong> that stays silent when you are right and raises an error when you are not (read it: that is your Stage 2 skill at work), and a hidden solution. Tick <em>Done</em> when the check prints <code>all checks passed</code>.</p><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e7a"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: an audit function</h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Turn the audit checklist into a function. Write <code>audit(df)</code> returning a dictionary with keys <code>rows</code>, <code>columns</code>, <code>missing_cells</code> (total missing values in the whole table), <code>duplicate_names</code> (rows whose <code>hgvs_pro</code> repeats an earlier one) and <code>by_type</code> (a dictionary of <code>record_type</code> counts). Run it on every new dataset from now on.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">audit</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">):</span>
    <span class="k">pass</span>   <span class="c1"># TODO</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">tiny</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">DataFrame</span><span class="p">({</span><span class="s2">"hgvs_pro"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"a"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"b"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"b"</span><span class="p">],</span> <span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="mf">1.0</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="kc">None</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mf">2.0</span><span class="p">],</span> <span class="s2">"record_type"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"x"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"x"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"y"</span><span class="p">]})</span>
<span class="n">r</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">audit</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">tiny</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">r</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"rows"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">3</span> <span class="ow">and</span> <span class="n">r</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"columns"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">3</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">r</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"missing_cells"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">1</span> <span class="ow">and</span> <span class="n">r</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"duplicate_names"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">1</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">r</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"by_type"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="p">{</span><span class="s2">"x"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"y"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">}</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">audit</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">))</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">audit</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">):</span>
<span class="w">    </span><span class="sd">"""Quick facts about a variant table."""</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="p">{</span>
        <span class="s2">"rows"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">),</span>
        <span class="s2">"columns"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">shape</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">],</span>
        <span class="s2">"missing_cells"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="nb">int</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">isna</span><span class="p">()</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">()</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">()),</span>
        <span class="s2">"duplicate_names"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="nb">int</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">duplicated</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">subset</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"hgvs_pro"</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">()),</span>
        <span class="s2">"by_type"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"record_type"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">value_counts</span><span class="p">()</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">to_dict</span><span class="p">(),</span>
    <span class="p">}</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e7b"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: split one variant name <span class="q-badge is-stretch">Stretch</span></h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Without pandas this time. Write <code>split_variant(name)</code> using <code>re.match</code> and the pattern from this stage. It returns a tuple <code>(ref, position, alt)</code> with the position as an <code>int</code>, or <code>None</code> if the name does not match.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">re</span>


<span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">split_variant</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="p">):</span>
    <span class="k">pass</span>   <span class="c1"># TODO</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">split_variant</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"p.Trp18Ter"</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"Trp"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">18</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"Ter"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">split_variant</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"p.Asn12="</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"Asn"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">12</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"="</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">split_variant</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"_wt"</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="ow">is</span> <span class="kc">None</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">split_variant</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"p.Gly12_Asp13del"</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="ow">is</span> <span class="kc">None</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">re</span>


<span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">split_variant</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="p">):</span>
<span class="w">    </span><span class="sd">"""Return (ref, position, alt) or None."""</span>
    <span class="n">m</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">re</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">match</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="sa">r</span><span class="s2">"^p\.([A-Za-z]</span><span class="si">{3}</span><span class="s2">)(\d+)([A-Za-z]</span><span class="si">{3}</span><span class="s2">|=)$"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">name</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">m</span> <span class="ow">is</span> <span class="kc">None</span><span class="p">:</span>
        <span class="k">return</span> <span class="kc">None</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">m</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">group</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">),</span> <span class="nb">int</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">m</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">group</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">)),</span> <span class="n">m</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">group</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div></details></div></div><div class="q-puzzles"><div class="q-puzzle" data-qid="s7p1" data-part="1"><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>First star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>How many <strong>rows</strong> does the table have (not counting the header)?</p></div><div class="q-answer"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s7p1">answer =</label><input id="in-s7p1" type="text" inputmode="decimal" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" autocapitalize="off" placeholder="a whole number"><button type="button" class="q-check">Check answer</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p><code>df.shape</code> gives <code>(rows, columns)</code>. <code>len(df)</code> gives the rows directly.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one way to get it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">))</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-puzzle is-part2" data-qid="s7p2" data-part="2" hidden><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>Second star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>After parsing <code>hgvs_pro</code>, how many <strong>distinct positions</strong> appear in the table? Ignore rows where no position could be read.</p></div><div class="q-answer"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s7p2">answer =</label><input id="in-s7p2" type="text" inputmode="decimal" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" autocapitalize="off" placeholder="a whole number"><button type="button" class="q-check">Check answer</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p><code>df["position"].nunique()</code> already skips missing values.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one way to get it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"position"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">nunique</span><span class="p">())</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-puzzle is-part2" data-qid="s7p3" data-part="3" data-kind="choices" data-hs="3b07bd79,cda83d7c,6fcda9b8" hidden><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>Third star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>Interpretation time. Pick the best answer for each.</p></div><div class="q-choices"><fieldset class="q-choice-set"><legend>A learner says: "a PTEN variant with score 0.9 is <em>lower</em> than a toy variant scoring 3.4, so the PTEN variant is worse." What is wrong?</legend><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s7p3-0" value="a"><span>Nothing; 0.9 is lower than 3.4.</span></label><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s7p3-0" value="b"><span>PTEN scores are wrong because they are below 1.</span></label><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s7p3-0" value="c"><span>The two scores come from different procedures and scales, so raw numbers cannot be compared across tables. Compare against each table's own reference points.</span></label></fieldset><fieldset class="q-choice-set"><legend>Why do analysts look at nonsense and synonymous variants before anything else?</legend><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s7p3-1" value="a"><span>They give reference points: nonsense should look broken and synonymous should look like the reference, so a surprise means something may be wrong.</span></label><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s7p3-1" value="b"><span>They are the most common variants.</span></label><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s7p3-1" value="c"><span>They are the only variants with scores.</span></label></fieldset><fieldset class="q-choice-set"><legend>A missense variant has a very low abundance score. What can you conclude?</legend><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s7p3-2" value="a"><span>It causes disease.</span></label><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s7p3-2" value="b"><span>The protein with this change is probably present at lower levels in the cell. Whether it affects function or health needs other evidence.</span></label><label class="q-choice"><input type="radio" name="s7p3-2" value="c"><span>It has no effect on PTEN.</span></label></fieldset></div><div class="q-answer is-choices"><button type="button" class="q-check">Check answers</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p>Question 1: ask what "score" means in each table. Question 3: abundance and activity are different things.</p></details></div></div></section>
<section class="q-stage" id="stage-8" data-stage="8"><header class="q-stage-head"><div class="q-stage-title"><span class="q-stage-num">Stage 7<span class="q-badge is-core">Core</span></span><h2>Region Rapids: group, merge and summarise</h2></div><div class="q-stage-stars" data-stars-for="8" aria-label="Stars earned in this stage"><span class="q-s" data-part="1">&#9733;</span><span class="q-s" data-part="2">&#9733;</span><span class="q-s" data-part="3">&#9733;</span></div></header><p class="q-promise">You will attach a second table to the first, summarise by position and by region, and learn why every summary needs a count next to it. <span class="q-time">About 40 minutes.</span></p><p class="q-colab-row"><a class="q-colab" href="https://colab.research.google.com/drive/{% endraw %}{{ site.data.colab.nb3 }}{% raw %}" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Open in Colab: Notebook 3: the PTEN data</a><span class="q-colab-note">Free Google account needed. Use File, Save a copy in Drive.</span></p><div class="q-prose"><p>Stage 6 gave you one row per variant. Questions usually live at a coarser level: <em>per position</em>, <em>per region</em>, <em>per category</em>. PTEN has two folded parts, a <strong>phosphatase domain</strong> (the enzyme part) and a <strong>C2 domain</strong> (which helps the protein attach to membranes), followed by a floppy <strong>tail</strong> with no fixed shape. A second table lists those stretches of the sequence with start and end positions:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">regions</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">read_csv</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">BASE</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="s2">"pten-region-annotations.csv"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">regions</span></code></pre></div><h3>Label each row with its region</h3><p>Write a small function that answers "which region contains this position?" for one position, then apply it to the whole column with <code>.map</code>:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">find_region</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">position</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">regions</span><span class="p">):</span>
    <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">isna</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">position</span><span class="p">):</span>
        <span class="k">return</span> <span class="s2">"no_position"</span>
    <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">r</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">regions</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">itertuples</span><span class="p">():</span>
        <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">r</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">start</span> <span class="o">&lt;=</span> <span class="n">position</span> <span class="o">&lt;=</span> <span class="n">r</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">end</span><span class="p">:</span>
            <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">r</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">region</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="s2">"other"</span>

<span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"region"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"position"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">map</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="k">lambda</span> <span class="n">pos</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="n">find_region</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">pos</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">regions</span><span class="p">))</span>
<span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"region"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">value_counts</span><span class="p">()</span></code></pre></div><aside class="q-callout is-trap"><strong class="q-callout-title">The missing-value guard is not optional</strong><p>Remove the <code>pd.isna</code> check and the function dies with <code>TypeError: boolean value of NA is ambiguous</code> on the first row without a position. Missing values do not behave like numbers; they need an explicit decision.</p></aside><p>That logic is reusable, so package it as a function too, next to <code>parse_hgvs</code>:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">function: label_regions</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">label_regions</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">regions</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">position_column</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"position"</span><span class="p">):</span>
<span class="w">    </span><span class="sd">"""Add a 'region' column from a table with region, start and end columns.</span>

<span class="sd">    Positions outside every region are labelled 'other'; rows without a</span>
<span class="sd">    position are labelled 'no_position'.</span>
<span class="sd">    """</span>
    <span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">find</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">position</span><span class="p">):</span>
        <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">isna</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">position</span><span class="p">):</span>
            <span class="k">return</span> <span class="s2">"no_position"</span>
        <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">r</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">regions</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">itertuples</span><span class="p">():</span>
            <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">r</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">start</span> <span class="o">&lt;=</span> <span class="n">position</span> <span class="o">&lt;=</span> <span class="n">r</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">end</span><span class="p">:</span>
                <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">r</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">region</span>
        <span class="k">return</span> <span class="s2">"other"</span>

    <span class="n">out</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">copy</span><span class="p">()</span>
    <span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"region"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">position_column</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">map</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">find</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">out</span></code></pre></div><h3>Focus the question</h3><p>Different record types answer different questions. For "how do single-letter changes affect abundance?" keep only <code>missense</code> rows and give that table a name:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">miss</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"record_type"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"missense"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">miss</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><h3>Summarise by region, with counts</h3><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">by_region</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">(</span>
    <span class="n">miss</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">groupby</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"region"</span><span class="p">)[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span>
        <span class="o">.</span><span class="n">agg</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">n</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"size"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">mean</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"mean"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">median</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"median"</span><span class="p">)</span>
        <span class="o">.</span><span class="n">round</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">by_region</span></code></pre></div><aside class="q-callout is-why"><strong class="q-callout-title">Always put the n next to the mean</strong><p>A mean of 3 variants and a mean of 3,000 look identical in a table. The count tells the reader how much weight to give each number. Median versus mean is worth looking at too: when they differ a lot, the distribution is lopsided, and a single number is hiding something.</p></aside><h3>Summarise by position, then merge back</h3><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">by_position</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">(</span>
    <span class="n">miss</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">groupby</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"position"</span><span class="p">)[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span>
        <span class="o">.</span><span class="n">agg</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">n_subs</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"size"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">mean_score</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"mean"</span><span class="p">)</span>
        <span class="o">.</span><span class="n">reset_index</span><span class="p">()</span>
<span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">by_position</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sort_values</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"mean_score"</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">head</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">10</span><span class="p">)</span>       <span class="c1"># the most affected positions</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">merged</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">merge</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">by_position</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">on</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"position"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">how</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"left"</span><span class="p">)</span>   <span class="c1"># adds n_subs and mean_score to every row</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">merged</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">),</span> <span class="s2">"a merge changed the number of rows!"</span>
<span class="n">merged</span><span class="p">[[</span><span class="s2">"hgvs_pro"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"n_subs"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"mean_score"</span><span class="p">]]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">head</span><span class="p">()</span></code></pre></div><p><code>how="left"</code> keeps every row of the left table and fills blanks where there is no match. The <code>assert</code> after a merge is cheap insurance: if the key is not unique on the right side, a merge silently multiplies rows.</p><aside class="q-callout is-note"><strong class="q-callout-title">Choosing a merge type</strong><ul><li><code>left</code>: keep everything from the table on the left (most common).</li><li><code>inner</code>: keep only rows that match on both sides.</li><li><code>outer</code>: keep everything from both.</li><li><code>validate="many_to_one"</code> makes pandas raise an error if your assumption about keys is wrong.</li></ul></aside><h3>Read the table like a scientist</h3><p>Numbers become findings only when you ask what they mean. Before you answer the last puzzle of this stage, look at your <code>by_region</code> table and ask three questions:</p><ol><li><strong>How big is each group?</strong> A median from 12 variants and one from 1,500 are not equally trustworthy.</li><li><strong>Does the pattern make biological sense?</strong> Folded domains need an exact shape, so changing their amino acids often destabilises them. A floppy tail has no shape to lose.</li><li><strong>What can abundance not tell you?</strong> A region can tolerate changes in abundance and still be essential for function.</li></ol></div><div class="q-practice"><h3>Practice: your turn</h3><p>Reading code teaches you what code looks like; writing it teaches you to code. Do these in your notebook. Each has a starter, a <strong>check cell</strong> that stays silent when you are right and raises an error when you are not (read it: that is your Stage 2 skill at work), and a hidden solution. Tick <em>Done</em> when the check prints <code>all checks passed</code>.</p><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e8a"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: a reusable group summary</h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Write <code>summarise_by(df, column)</code> that keeps only <code>missense</code> rows, groups by <code>column</code>, and returns a DataFrame with columns <code>n</code>, <code>mean</code> and <code>median</code> of the score. One function now answers "by region?", "by new letters?", "by anything?".</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">summarise_by</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">column</span><span class="p">):</span>
    <span class="k">pass</span>   <span class="c1"># TODO</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">t</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">summarise_by</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"region"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="nb">list</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">t</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">columns</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"n"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"mean"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"median"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">t</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"n"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">()</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"record_type"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"missense"</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">()</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="mi">19</span> <span class="o">&lt;=</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">summarise_by</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"alt"</span><span class="p">))</span> <span class="o">&lt;=</span> <span class="mi">20</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">summarise_by</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">column</span><span class="p">):</span>
<span class="w">    </span><span class="sd">"""n, mean and median missense score per group."""</span>
    <span class="n">miss</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"record_type"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"missense"</span><span class="p">]</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">miss</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">groupby</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">column</span><span class="p">)[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">agg</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">n</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"size"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">mean</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"mean"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">median</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"median"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e8b"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: the most sensitive positions <span class="q-badge is-stretch">Stretch</span></h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Among positions with <strong>at least 15 missense variants</strong>, find the <strong>10 positions with the lowest mean missense score</strong>. Store them, lowest first, in a list <code>most_sensitive</code>. Your <code>miss</code> table from this stage is the starting point.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">most_sensitive</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[]</span>   <span class="c1"># TODO: groupby position, filter on n, sort, head(10)</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">g</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">miss</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">groupby</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"position"</span><span class="p">)[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">agg</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">n</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"size"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">mean</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"mean"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">g</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">g</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">g</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"n"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">&gt;=</span> <span class="mi">15</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sort_values</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"mean"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">most_sensitive</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">10</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="nb">int</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">p</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">p</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">most_sensitive</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="nb">int</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">p</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">p</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">g</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">index</span><span class="p">[:</span><span class="mi">10</span><span class="p">]]</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">g</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">miss</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">groupby</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"position"</span><span class="p">)[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">agg</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">n</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"size"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">mean</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"mean"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">g</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">g</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">g</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"n"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">&gt;=</span> <span class="mi">15</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sort_values</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"mean"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">most_sensitive</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="nb">list</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">g</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">index</span><span class="p">[:</span><span class="mi">10</span><span class="p">])</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e8c"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: attach the notes <span class="q-badge is-stretch">Stretch</span></h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">The regions table has an <code>annotation</code> column with a plain-English description. Merge it onto <code>df</code> through the <code>region</code> column so every row gets its description (rows with no region stay, with a blank). Call the result <code>with_notes</code>, and make sure the row count did not change.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">with_notes</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="kc">None</span>   <span class="c1"># TODO: df.merge(...)</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">assert</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">with_notes</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="s2">"annotation"</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">with_notes</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">columns</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">with_notes</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">loc</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">with_notes</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"region"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"phosphatase_domain"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"annotation"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">notna</span><span class="p">()</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">all</span><span class="p">()</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">with_notes</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">merge</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">regions</span><span class="p">[[</span><span class="s2">"region"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"annotation"</span><span class="p">]],</span> <span class="n">on</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"region"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">how</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"left"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div></details></div></div><div class="q-puzzles"><div class="q-puzzle" data-qid="s8p1" data-part="1"><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>First star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>Using only <code>missense</code> rows, what is the mean <code>score</code> of variants in the <code>phosphatase_domain</code> region, to 3 decimal places? (Regions include both their start and end positions.)</p></div><div class="q-answer"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s8p1">answer =</label><input id="in-s8p1" type="text" inputmode="decimal" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" autocapitalize="off" placeholder="3 decimal places"><button type="button" class="q-check">Check answer</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p>Parse positions, label regions, filter on <code>record_type == "missense"</code> and <code>region == "phosphatase_domain"</code>, then <code>.mean()</code> of <code>score</code>. If your number includes nonsense or synonymous rows, it will be off.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one way to get it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">sub</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"record_type"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"missense"</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">&amp;</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"region"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"phosphatase_domain"</span><span class="p">)]</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="nb">round</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">sub</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">mean</span><span class="p">(),</span> <span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">),</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">sub</span><span class="p">))</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-puzzle is-part2" data-qid="s8p2" data-part="2" hidden><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>Second star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>Among the three annotated regions (<code>phosphatase_domain</code>, <code>c2_domain</code>, <code>disordered_tail</code>), which has the <strong>lowest median</strong> missense <code>score</code>? Write the region name.</p></div><div class="q-answer"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s8p2">answer =</label><input id="in-s8p2" type="text" inputmode="text" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" autocapitalize="off" placeholder="region_name"><button type="button" class="q-check">Check answer</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p>The <code>by_region</code> table above has the medians. Ignore the rows named <code>other</code> and <code>no_position</code>, then look at the smallest median.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one way to get it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">by_region</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">miss</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">groupby</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"region"</span><span class="p">)[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">median</span><span class="p">()</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">by_region</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">drop</span><span class="p">([</span><span class="s2">"other"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"no_position"</span><span class="p">],</span> <span class="n">errors</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"ignore"</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">idxmin</span><span class="p">())</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-puzzle is-part2" data-qid="s8p3" data-part="3" data-kind="reflect" data-minwords="45" hidden><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>Third star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p><strong>Write your interpretation</strong> (about four sentences). Using your own <code>by_region</code> table, say: which region looks most and which least tolerant of changes, whether that makes biological sense for a folded domain versus a floppy tail, and how the <strong>number of variants in each region</strong> affects how much you trust the comparison.</p></div><div class="q-answer is-paste"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s8p3">your conclusion</label><textarea id="in-s8p3" rows="7" spellcheck="true" autocomplete="off" placeholder="The ... has the lowest median (value, n = ...), while the ..."></textarea><button type="button" class="q-check">Submit my conclusion</button></div><div class="q-model" hidden><h4>A model answer (use your own numbers)</h4><p>"The ___ has the lowest median missense score (___, n = ___) and the ___ the highest (___, n = ___). That fits the idea that folded domains must keep a precise shape, so many amino acid swaps there destabilise the protein, while a disordered tail has no fixed structure to lose and is more forgiving. The regions have very different numbers of variants, so a median from a small group is less reliable and I would not over-read a small gap. Also, abundance is only one property: a region that keeps the protein stable might still matter for activity."</p><h4>Did you cover</h4><ul><li>Named the extreme regions with a number and an n?</li><li>Gave a biological reason and judged whether it fits?</li><li>Said how group size changes your confidence?</li><li>Noted what abundance cannot tell you?</li></ul></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? How to start</summary><p>Start from the numbers: name the regions, quote a median and an n for each. Then give one biological reason and one reason to be cautious.</p></details></div></div></section>
<section class="q-stage" id="stage-9" data-stage="9"><header class="q-stage-head"><div class="q-stage-title"><span class="q-stage-num">Stage 8<span class="q-badge is-core">Core</span></span><h2>Histogram Meadow: plots for distributions</h2></div><div class="q-stage-stars" data-stars-for="9" aria-label="Stars earned in this stage"><span class="q-s" data-part="1">&#9733;</span><span class="q-s" data-part="2">&#9733;</span></div></header><p class="q-promise">Seven plots for the question every experimentalist asks first: what does my data look like, and do my groups differ? You will learn when each one is the right choice. <span class="q-time">About 45 minutes.</span></p><p class="q-colab-row"><a class="q-colab" href="https://colab.research.google.com/drive/{% endraw %}{{ site.data.colab.nb4 }}{% raw %}" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Open in Colab: Notebook 4: plots</a><span class="q-colab-note">Free Google account needed. Use File, Save a copy in Drive.</span></p><div class="q-prose"><p>Open notebook 4 in Colab (button above). Its first cells reload the data and define the helper functions from earlier stages, so it works in a fresh session. The setup looks like this:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">first cells</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pandas</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="k">as</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pd</span>
<span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pathlib</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">Path</span>
<span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">matplotlib.pyplot</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="k">as</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">plt</span>
<span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">seaborn</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="k">as</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">sns</span>

<span class="n">BASE</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="s2">"https://atharva-bhagwat.github.io/assets/data/"</span>
<span class="n">df</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">read_csv</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">BASE</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="s2">"pten-variant-abundance.csv"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">regions</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">read_csv</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">BASE</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="s2">"pten-region-annotations.csv"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">df</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">label_regions</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">parse_hgvs</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">),</span> <span class="n">regions</span><span class="p">)</span>    <span class="c1"># the two helpers from Stages 6 and 7</span>

<span class="n">sns</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">set_theme</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">style</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"whitegrid"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">context</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"notebook"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">palette</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"colorblind"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">TYPES</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"synonymous"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"missense"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"nonsense"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="n">data</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"record_type"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">isin</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">TYPES</span><span class="p">)]</span>

<span class="n">FIGS</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">Path</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"figures"</span><span class="p">)</span>          <span class="c1"># in Colab: the Files panel (folder icon) shows it</span>
<span class="n">FIGS</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">mkdir</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">exist_ok</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">True</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><h3>Two libraries, one mental model</h3><p><a href="https://matplotlib.org/stable/index.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener">matplotlib</a> is the foundation: a <strong>figure</strong> is the canvas and an <strong>axes</strong> is one chart on it. <a href="https://seaborn.pydata.org/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">seaborn</a> sits on top and draws statistical plots from a DataFrame in one line. The pattern you will repeat all stage:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">the pattern</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">fig</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ax</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">plt</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">subplots</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">figsize</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">6</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">4</span><span class="p">))</span>        <span class="c1"># make a canvas with one chart</span>
<span class="n">sns</span><span class="o">.&lt;</span><span class="n">something</span><span class="o">&gt;</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">data</span><span class="o">=...</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">x</span><span class="o">=...</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">y</span><span class="o">=...</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ax</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">ax</span><span class="p">)</span>    <span class="c1"># draw onto that chart</span>
<span class="n">ax</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">set</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">xlabel</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"..."</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ylabel</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"..."</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">title</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"..."</span><span class="p">)</span>   <span class="c1"># label it</span></code></pre></div><aside class="q-callout is-tip"><strong class="q-callout-title">Name your axes</strong><p>A plot without axis labels and units is a rumour. Add <code>xlabel</code> and <code>ylabel</code> every time, even in exploration. It also forces you to know what the plot says.</p></aside><h3>Which plot answers which question?</h3><div class="q-table-wrap"><table class="q-table"><thead><tr><th>Question</th><th>Plot</th><th>Watch out for</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>What is the shape of one variable?</td><td><strong>Histogram</strong></td><td>The number of bins changes the picture. Try 20, 40 and 80.</td></tr><tr><td>Same, smoothed, for several groups</td><td><strong>KDE</strong> (density)</td><td>Smoothing can invent bumps and spill past the real range.</td></tr><tr><td>Compare distributions without choosing bins</td><td><strong>ECDF</strong></td><td>Less familiar, but the most honest. Read it as "what fraction is below this value".</td></tr><tr><td>Compare group centres and spread</td><td><strong>Box plot</strong></td><td>Hides the shape. Two humps look like one box.</td></tr><tr><td>Compare group shapes</td><td><strong>Violin</strong></td><td>Needs plenty of points per group.</td></tr><tr><td>Show every observation</td><td><strong>Strip plot</strong></td><td>Overlapping points. Use <code>alpha</code> and a small <code>size</code>.</td></tr><tr><td>How many of each category?</td><td><strong>Count / bar plot</strong></td><td>Start bars at zero. Always.</td></tr></tbody></table></div><h3>The gallery</h3><p>Each cell below is a complete plot. Run them, change one thing, run again.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">1. histogram</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">fig</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ax</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">plt</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">subplots</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">figsize</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">6</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">4</span><span class="p">))</span>
<span class="n">sns</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">histplot</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">data</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">data</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">data</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"record_type"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"missense"</span><span class="p">],</span> <span class="n">x</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">bins</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">40</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ax</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">ax</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">ax</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">set</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">xlabel</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"abundance score"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ylabel</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"number of variants"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">title</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"Histogram: missense variants"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">2. density (KDE)</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">fig</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ax</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">plt</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">subplots</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">figsize</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">6</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">4</span><span class="p">))</span>
<span class="n">sns</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">kdeplot</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">data</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">data</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">x</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">hue</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"record_type"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">hue_order</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">TYPES</span><span class="p">,</span>
            <span class="n">common_norm</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">False</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">fill</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">True</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">alpha</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mf">0.3</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ax</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">ax</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">ax</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">set</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">xlabel</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"abundance score"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">title</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"KDE: one curve per record type"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">3. ECDF</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">fig</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ax</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">plt</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">subplots</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">figsize</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">6</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">4</span><span class="p">))</span>
<span class="n">sns</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">ecdfplot</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">data</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">data</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">x</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">hue</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"record_type"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">hue_order</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">TYPES</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ax</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">ax</span><span class="p">)</span>
