<span class="n">sweep</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">round</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">cell</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">fig</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">axes</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">plt</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">subplots</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">figsize</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">9</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mf">3.5</span><span class="p">))</span>
<span class="n">sns</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">lineplot</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">data</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">sweep</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">x</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"min_expts"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">y</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"n_variants"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">marker</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"o"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ax</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">axes</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">])</span>
<span class="n">sns</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">lineplot</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">data</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">sweep</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">x</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"min_expts"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">y</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"mean_score"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">marker</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"o"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ax</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">axes</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">])</span>
<span class="n">axes</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">set</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">xlabel</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"minimum experiments"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ylabel</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"variants kept"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">axes</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">set</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">xlabel</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"minimum experiments"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ylabel</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"mean missense score"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">fig</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">tight_layout</span><span class="p">()</span></code></pre></div><p>If the mean barely moves while the count falls, the conclusion is robust to this rule. If it swings, say so in your write-up. Either answer is a result.</p><aside class="q-callout is-why"><strong class="q-callout-title">This is the heart of reproducible research</strong><p>Readers should be able to change the dial themselves. A function with a documented parameter lets them. A hand-edited spreadsheet does not.</p></aside><h3>Write the conclusion</h3><p>A number is not a conclusion. A conclusion says what you found, how sure you are, and what could change it. Look at your sweep: how many variants does each rule keep, and how far does the mean move?</p></div><div class="q-practice"><h3>Practice: your turn</h3><p>Reading code teaches you what code looks like; writing it teaches you to code. Do these in your notebook. Each has a starter, a <strong>check cell</strong> that stays silent when you are right and raises an error when you are not (read it: that is your Stage 2 skill at work), and a hidden solution. Tick <em>Done</em> when the check prints <code>all checks passed</code>.</p><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e11a"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: sweep as a function</h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Write <code>sweep(df, ks)</code> that calls <code>summarise</code> for every value in <code>ks</code> and returns a DataFrame with columns <code>min_expts</code>, <code>n_variants</code>, <code>mean_score</code>. Then call it with <code>[1, 3, 5, 7]</code> and plot <code>mean_score</code> against <code>min_expts</code>.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">sweep</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ks</span><span class="p">):</span>
    <span class="k">pass</span>   <span class="c1"># TODO</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">s</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">sweep</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">5</span><span class="p">])</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="nb">list</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">s</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">columns</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"min_expts"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"n_variants"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"mean_score"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">s</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">3</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">s</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"n_variants"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">is_monotonic_decreasing</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">sweep</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ks</span><span class="p">):</span>
<span class="w">    </span><span class="sd">"""Mean missense score in the phosphatase domain for each min_expts."""</span>
    <span class="n">rows</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[]</span>
    <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">k</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">ks</span><span class="p">:</span>
        <span class="n">r</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">summarise</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">min_expts</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">k</span><span class="p">)</span>
        <span class="n">rows</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">append</span><span class="p">({</span><span class="s2">"min_expts"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="n">k</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"n_variants"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="nb">int</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">r</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"count"</span><span class="p">]),</span> <span class="s2">"mean_score"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="n">r</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"mean"</span><span class="p">]})</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">DataFrame</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">rows</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div></details></div></div><div class="q-puzzles"><div class="q-puzzle" data-qid="s11p1" data-part="1"><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>First star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>Keep only rows whose <code>expts</code> is <strong>at least 3</strong>, across the whole table (every record type). How many rows remain?</p></div><div class="q-answer"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s11p1">answer =</label><input id="in-s11p1" type="text" inputmode="decimal" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" autocapitalize="off" placeholder="a whole number"><button type="button" class="q-check">Check answer</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p><code>(df["expts"] &gt;= 3).sum()</code> or <code>len(df[df["expts"] &gt;= 3])</code>. Rows with a missing <code>expts</code> fail the comparison and are not counted.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one way to get it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">((</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"expts"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">&gt;=</span> <span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">())</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-puzzle is-part2" data-qid="s11p2" data-part="2" hidden><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>Second star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>Use <code>summarise</code> for the <code>phosphatase_domain</code>. Compute the mean missense score <strong>with</strong> the rule <code>min_expts=3</code> minus the mean <strong>without</strong> it. Give the difference (after minus before) to 3 decimal places; keep the sign.</p></div><div class="q-answer"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s11p2">answer =</label><input id="in-s11p2" type="text" inputmode="decimal" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" autocapitalize="off" placeholder="e.g. -0.012"><button type="button" class="q-check">Check answer</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p><code>summarise(df, min_expts=3)["mean"] - summarise(df)["mean"]</code>. A tiny difference is good news for the robustness of the earlier answer.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one way to get it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="n">after</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">summarise</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">min_expts</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">)[</span><span class="s2">"mean"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="n">before</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">summarise</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">)[</span><span class="s2">"mean"</span><span class="p">]</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="nb">round</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">after</span> <span class="o">-</span> <span class="n">before</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">))</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-puzzle is-part2" data-qid="s11p3" data-part="3" data-kind="reflect" data-minwords="55" hidden><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>Third star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p><strong>Write a short conclusion</strong> (four to six sentences) about the phosphatase domain, as you would in a lab meeting. Include the mean with and without the rule, how many variants each rule keeps, whether you think the conclusion is robust to this choice, and one other analysis choice that could change it.</p></div><div class="q-answer is-paste"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s11p3">your conclusion</label><textarea id="in-s11p3" rows="7" spellcheck="true" autocomplete="off" placeholder="Using all variants, the mean missense score in the phosphatase domain is ... After requiring at least 3 experiments ..."></textarea><button type="button" class="q-check">Submit my conclusion</button></div><div class="q-model" hidden><h4>A model answer (use your own numbers)</h4><p>"Across all ___ missense variants in the phosphatase domain the mean score is ___. Requiring at least 3 experiments keeps ___ variants and moves the mean to ___ (a change of ___). Because the shift is [small / large] compared with the spread between variants, I consider the domain-level conclusion [robust / sensitive] to this rule. The sweep shows the estimate [stabilises / keeps changing] as the threshold rises, but fewer variants means a noisier mean. Other choices could matter too, such as excluding variants with large standard errors, or using the median instead of the mean, so I would check those next before making any strong claim."</p><h4>Did you cover</h4><ul><li>Both means and both counts?</li><li>A judgement about robustness, with a reason?</li><li>The cost of keeping fewer variants?</li><li>A different choice that could change the result?</li></ul></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? How to start</summary><p>Use the sentence frame: finding with numbers, then how it changed, then your confidence, then a caveat or next check.</p></details></div></div></section>
<div class="q-part"><span class="q-part-kicker">Part 2</span><h2>Take it to your own computer</h2><p>Once you have seen what notebooks can do, the limits become obvious. Now set up a proper workspace, learn the terminal, organise a project, and scale up on the cluster.</p></div>
<section class="q-stage" id="stage-0" data-stage="0"><header class="q-stage-head"><div class="q-stage-title"><span class="q-stage-num">Stage 11<span class="q-badge is-optional">Level up</span></span><h2>Basecamp: set up your tools</h2></div><div class="q-stage-stars" data-stars-for="0" aria-label="Stars earned in this stage"><span class="q-s" data-part="1">&#9733;</span><span class="q-s" data-part="2">&#9733;</span></div></header><p class="q-promise">By the end you will have an isolated Python environment, an editor that understands it, and proof that both talk to each other. <span class="q-time">About 60 minutes.</span></p><div class="q-prose"><p>You have been writing code in Colab, where everything is ready-made. That is the best way to learn, but it has limits: sessions reset and your files disappear, you cannot easily run a script on a schedule, big datasets do not fit, and you cannot rebuild exactly the same setup next year. Your own computer fixes all of those, and it needs a little setup, once.</p><p>Most beginner pain is not about Python at all. It is about <em>which</em> Python is running, and where. So we fix that first, once, properly.</p><h3>Terminal first aid</h3><p>The <strong>terminal</strong> is a window where you type commands instead of clicking. It looks unfriendly but it only does what you tell it, and it never breaks anything you did not ask it to. Open it now: <strong>Terminal</strong> on macOS (press <kbd>Cmd</kbd>+<kbd>Space</kbd>, type Terminal), <strong>PowerShell</strong> on Windows (Start menu, type PowerShell). Each line you type ends with <kbd>Enter</kbd>.</p><p>A terminal is always "standing" in one folder, called the <strong>working directory</strong>. These five commands cover 90 percent of what you will ever need:</p><div class="q-table-wrap"><table class="q-table"><thead><tr><th>Command</th><th>What it does</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><code>pwd</code></td><td>Print where you are standing now (on Windows PowerShell: also <code>pwd</code>).</td></tr><tr><td><code>ls</code></td><td>List what is in this folder (Windows PowerShell understands <code>ls</code> too).</td></tr><tr><td><code>cd folder_name</code></td><td>Step into a folder. <code>cd ..</code> steps back out to the parent folder.</td></tr><tr><td><code>mkdir new_folder</code></td><td>Make a new folder.</td></tr><tr><td><code>python script.py</code></td><td>Run a Python file (once Python is installed, below).</td></tr></tbody></table></div><ul><li>Press <kbd>Tab</kbd> after typing the first letters of a name and the terminal completes it. It saves typing and typos.</li><li>Press <kbd>&uarr;</kbd> to bring back the previous command. Press <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>C</kbd> to stop something that is running.</li><li>Copy a command, right-click or <kbd>Cmd</kbd>/<kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>V</kbd> to paste, then Enter. A command that prints nothing usually worked.</li><li>An error message is not a disaster: read the last line, exactly as in Stage 2.</li></ul><p>Do the <strong>terminal drill</strong> in the practice section at the end of this stage before you start the install steps.</p><h3>1. Install VS Code</h3><p>Download it from <a href="https://code.visualstudio.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">code.visualstudio.com</a> and run the installer. VS Code is a text editor that grows into an IDE when you add extensions. It will be where you write scripts, run notebooks, browse files, and use a terminal, all in one window.</p><aside class="q-callout is-tip"><strong class="q-callout-title">macOS only: enable the <code>code</code> command</strong><p>Open VS Code, press <kbd>Cmd</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>P</kbd>, type <code>Shell Command: Install 'code' command in PATH</code> and press Enter. Windows and Linux installers usually do this for you.</p></aside><h3>2. Install Miniconda from the command line</h3><p>conda is a program that creates <strong>environments</strong>: separate folders, each with its own Python and its own set of libraries. Project A can use pandas 2 while project B uses pandas 3, and neither can break the other. <strong>Miniconda</strong> is the small installer: just conda and Python. We install it entirely from the terminal. Open a terminal first: Terminal on macOS or Linux, <strong>PowerShell</strong> on Windows (Start menu, type PowerShell).</p><p>Pick your system. On a Mac, check which chip you have with <code>uname -m</code>: <code>arm64</code> means Apple silicon, <code>x86_64</code> means Intel.</p><div class="q-os" data-os-group><div class="q-os-tabs" role="tablist" aria-label="Operating system"><button type="button" role="tab" data-os="mac">macOS / Linux</button><button type="button" role="tab" data-os="win">Windows</button></div><div class="q-os-pane" data-os-pane="mac"><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">macOS / Linux terminal</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code>uname<span class="w"> </span>-m

<span class="c1"># macOS, Apple silicon (arm64):</span>
<span class="nv">URL</span><span class="o">=</span>https://repo.anaconda.com/miniconda/Miniconda3-latest-MacOSX-arm64.sh
<span class="c1"># macOS, Intel (x86_64): use this line instead</span>
<span class="c1"># URL=https://repo.anaconda.com/miniconda/Miniconda3-latest-MacOSX-x86_64.sh</span>
<span class="c1"># Linux (x86_64): use this line instead</span>
<span class="c1"># URL=https://repo.anaconda.com/miniconda/Miniconda3-latest-Linux-x86_64.sh</span>

mkdir<span class="w"> </span>-p<span class="w"> </span>~/miniconda3
curl<span class="w"> </span>-L<span class="w"> </span><span class="nv">$URL</span><span class="w"> </span>-o<span class="w"> </span>~/miniconda3/miniconda.sh
bash<span class="w"> </span>~/miniconda3/miniconda.sh<span class="w"> </span>-b<span class="w"> </span>-u<span class="w"> </span>-p<span class="w"> </span>~/miniconda3
rm<span class="w"> </span>~/miniconda3/miniconda.sh

~/miniconda3/bin/conda<span class="w"> </span>init<span class="w"> </span>bash<span class="w"> </span>zsh</code></pre></div></div><div class="q-os-pane" data-os-pane="win"><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">Windows (PowerShell)</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="nb">Invoke-WebRequest</span> <span class="n">-Uri</span> <span class="s2">"https://repo.anaconda.com/miniconda/Miniconda3-latest-Windows-x86_64.exe"</span> <span class="n">-OutFile</span> <span class="s2">".\miniconda.exe"</span>
<span class="nb">Start-Process</span> <span class="n">-FilePath</span> <span class="s2">".\miniconda.exe"</span> <span class="n">-ArgumentList</span> <span class="s2">"/InstallationType=JustMe"</span><span class="p">,</span><span class="s2">"/RegisterPython=0"</span><span class="p">,</span><span class="s2">"/S"</span><span class="p">,</span><span class="s2">"/D=$env:USERPROFILE\miniconda3"</span> <span class="n">-Wait</span>
<span class="nb">Remove-Item</span> <span class="s2">".\miniconda.exe"</span>

<span class="p">&amp;</span> <span class="s2">"$env:USERPROFILE\miniconda3\Scripts\conda.exe"</span> <span class="n">init</span> <span class="n">powershell</span></code></pre></div></div></div><p>The last line connects conda to your shell. <strong>Close the terminal completely and open a new one</strong>. The prompt should now start with <code>(base)</code>. Then check:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">terminal</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code>conda<span class="w"> </span>--version
which<span class="w"> </span>python<span class="w">    </span><span class="c1"># Windows: Get-Command python</span></code></pre></div><aside class="q-callout is-trap"><strong class="q-callout-title">Windows: "running scripts is disabled on this system"</strong><p>PowerShell blocks the startup file conda adds. Run <code>Set-ExecutionPolicy -Scope CurrentUser RemoteSigned</code>, answer Y, then open a new PowerShell window.</p></aside><aside class="q-callout is-trap"><strong class="q-callout-title">conda says something about "Terms of Service"</strong><p>Newer conda versions can stop and ask you to accept channel terms before installing packages. The message prints the exact command to run. Copy it, run it, then repeat your original command. This is your first lesson in reading error messages: the answer was in the message.</p></aside><p>Optional but pleasant: stop conda from activating <code>base</code> in every new terminal.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">terminal</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code>conda<span class="w"> </span>config<span class="w"> </span>--set<span class="w"> </span>auto_activate_base<span class="w"> </span><span class="nb">false</span></code></pre></div><h3>3. Create the environment</h3><p>One command makes a fresh environment called <code>pandas-quest</code> with everything this guide needs:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">terminal</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code>conda<span class="w"> </span>create<span class="w"> </span>-n<span class="w"> </span>pandas-quest<span class="w"> </span>-c<span class="w"> </span>conda-forge<span class="w"> </span><span class="nv">python</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="m">3</span>.12<span class="w"> </span>pandas<span class="w"> </span>numpy<span class="w"> </span>matplotlib<span class="w"> </span>seaborn<span class="w"> </span>scipy<span class="w"> </span>ipykernel<span class="w"> </span>-y</code></pre></div><p>Then step inside it. You will do this every time you open a new terminal:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">terminal</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code>conda<span class="w"> </span>activate<span class="w"> </span>pandas-quest</code></pre></div><p>Your prompt now starts with <code>(pandas-quest)</code>. That prefix is your seatbelt light: if it is missing, you are not in the environment.</p><h3>4. Make a project folder and open it</h3><div class="q-os" data-os-group><div class="q-os-tabs" role="tablist" aria-label="Operating system"><button type="button" role="tab" data-os="mac">macOS / Linux</button><button type="button" role="tab" data-os="win">Windows</button></div><div class="q-os-pane" data-os-pane="mac"><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">macOS / Linux terminal</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code>mkdir<span class="w"> </span>pandas-quest
<span class="nb">cd</span><span class="w"> </span>pandas-quest
code<span class="w"> </span>.</code></pre></div></div><div class="q-os-pane" data-os-pane="win"><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">Windows (Anaconda Prompt)</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code>mkdir<span class="w"> </span>pandas-quest
<span class="nb">cd</span><span class="w"> </span>pandas-quest
code<span class="w"> </span>.</code></pre></div></div></div><p>VS Code opens with that folder. Always open the <em>folder</em>, not single files. It is what lets VS Code find your data, scripts and environment.</p><h3>5. Add extensions</h3><p>An <strong>extension</strong> is an add-on that teaches VS Code new skills. You install them from inside VS Code, and you will use the same few clicks every time you want a new one.</p><ol><li>Click the <strong>Extensions</strong> icon in the vertical bar on the far left of the window. It looks like four squares with one flying off. No icon bar? Use the menu: <strong>View</strong>, then <strong>Extensions</strong>. Keyboard shortcut: <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>X</kbd> (Windows, Linux) or <kbd>Cmd</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>X</kbd> (Mac).</li><li>Click the search box at the top of the panel. It says <em>Search Extensions in Marketplace</em>. The Marketplace is VS Code's app store, and everything in it is free to install.</li><li>Type the name from the table below, for example <code>Python</code>. Results appear underneath, most popular first.</li><li>Pick the right result: check the <strong>publisher name</strong> under the title, and the download count (the good ones have millions or hundreds of thousands). Click the result to read its description on the right.</li><li>Click the blue <strong>Install</strong> button. Wait a few seconds until it changes to a gear icon. That means it worked. If a <strong>Reload</strong> or <strong>Restart Extensions</strong> button appears, click it.</li><li>Repeat for each extension in the table.</li></ol><div class="q-table-wrap"><table class="q-table"><thead><tr><th>Search for</th><th>Publisher</th><th>What it does for you</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><strong>Python</strong></td><td>Microsoft</td><td>Runs and debugs <code>.py</code> files, finds your conda environments, adds autocomplete.</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Jupyter</strong></td><td>Microsoft</td><td>Opens <code>.ipynb</code> notebooks inside VS Code and runs cells.</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Rainbow CSV</strong></td><td>mechatroner</td><td>Colours each column of a CSV differently so you can read raw data files.</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Data Wrangler</strong></td><td>Microsoft</td><td>Opens a DataFrame as a scrollable grid with summary stats.</td></tr><tr><td><strong>Error Lens</strong></td><td>Alexander (usernamehw)</td><td>Prints errors and warnings at the end of the offending line. Great for making errors feel normal.</td></tr></tbody></table></div><aside class="q-callout is-tip"><strong class="q-callout-title">Find an exact extension with its ID</strong><p>Every extension has a unique ID. Typing it with <code>@id:</code> in front shows exactly one result, so you cannot pick a look-alike. Try <code>@id:ms-python.python</code>. The IDs for this guide are <code>ms-python.python</code>, <code>ms-toolsai.jupyter</code>, <code>mechatroner.rainbow-csv</code>, <code>ms-toolsai.datawrangler</code> and <code>usernamehw.errorlens</code>.</p></aside><aside class="q-callout is-trap"><strong class="q-callout-title">Look-alikes</strong><p>Searching <code>Python</code> returns many extensions. Choose the one published by <strong>Microsoft</strong> with the blue verified checkmark next to the publisher name and the largest download count. Installing the Python extension also adds a few helpers (such as Pylance) automatically. That is normal.</p></aside><p>To confirm everything is installed, type <code>@installed</code> in the search box. You should see all five in the list. The same box shows other useful filters: <code>@recommended</code> for suggestions, and the clock icon for recently updated extensions.</p><h3>6. Tell VS Code which Python to use</h3><p>Press <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>/<kbd>Cmd</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>P</kbd>, type <code>Python: Select Interpreter</code>, and choose the one named <code>pandas-quest</code>. Its path contains <code>envs/pandas-quest</code>. A notebook has its own picker, which you will use in the next stage.</p><h3>7. Run the check script</h3><p>In VS Code create a new file named <code>check_setup.py</code> (File, New File), paste this in, save, and run it from the terminal with <code>python check_setup.py</code>.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check_setup.py</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">sys</span>
<span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">platform</span>

<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"python    "</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">platform</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">python_version</span><span class="p">())</span>
<span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">name</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"pandas"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"numpy"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"matplotlib"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"seaborn"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"scipy"</span><span class="p">]:</span>
    <span class="n">module</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="nb">__import__</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="sa">f</span><span class="s2">"</span><span class="si">{</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="si">:</span><span class="s2">&lt;10</span><span class="si">}</span><span class="s2">"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">module</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">__version__</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"env       "</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">sys</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">prefix</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">replace</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"</span><span class="se">\\</span><span class="s2">"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"/"</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">split</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"/"</span><span class="p">)[</span><span class="o">-</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">])</span></code></pre></div><p>You should see something shaped like this (your version numbers will differ):</p><div class="qcode is-output"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">example output</span></div><pre><code>python     3.12.8
pandas     3.0.1
numpy      2.3.5
matplotlib 3.10.7
seaborn    0.13.2
scipy      1.16.3
env        pandas-quest</code></pre></div><aside class="q-callout is-trap"><strong class="q-callout-title"><code>ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'pandas'</code></strong><p>Python is running, but it is not the one inside <code>pandas-quest</code>. Check for the <code>(pandas-quest)</code> prefix in your terminal, and re-run <code>Python: Select Interpreter</code>. Nine times out of ten, this is the whole story.</p></aside></div><div class="q-practice"><h3>Practice: your turn</h3><p>Reading code teaches you what code looks like; writing it teaches you to code. Do these in your notebook. Each has a starter, a <strong>check cell</strong> that stays silent when you are right and raises an error when you are not (read it: that is your Stage 2 skill at work), and a hidden solution. Tick <em>Done</em> when the check prints <code>all checks passed</code>.</p><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e0a"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: terminal drill</h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Open your terminal and, using only the commands from the table, do this in order: (1) print where you are; (2) make a folder called <code>quest-drill</code>; (3) step into it; (4) make two folders inside it called <code>data</code> and <code>results</code>; (5) list what is there (you should see both); (6) step back out to where you started and print where you are. There is no check cell: the check is that <code>ls</code> showed <code>data</code> and <code>results</code> and that the last <code>pwd</code> matches the first.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">your commands</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="nb">pwd</span>
<span class="c1"># your commands here</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="nb">pwd</span>
mkdir<span class="w"> </span>quest-drill
<span class="nb">cd</span><span class="w"> </span>quest-drill
mkdir<span class="w"> </span>data<span class="w"> </span>results
ls
<span class="nb">cd</span><span class="w"> </span>..
<span class="nb">pwd</span></code></pre></div></details></div></div><div class="q-puzzles"><div class="q-puzzle" data-qid="s0p1" data-part="1"><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>First star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>Paste everything <code>check_setup.py</code> printed. The checker looks for Python, pandas, matplotlib and seaborn versions, and an <code>env</code> line that says <code>pandas-quest</code>.</p></div><div class="q-answer is-paste"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s0p1">paste output</label><textarea id="in-s0p1" rows="6" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" placeholder="python     3.12.x
pandas     x.y.z
..."></textarea><button type="button" class="q-check">Check output</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p>If a library line is missing, the install command in step 3 did not finish. Run it again and read the last lines it prints.</p></details></div><div class="q-puzzle is-part2" data-qid="s0p2" data-part="2" hidden><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>Second star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>Now prove VS Code itself uses that environment. In the next stage you will create a notebook; for now, any notebook will do (<kbd>Ctrl</kbd>/<kbd>Cmd</kbd>+<kbd>Shift</kbd>+<kbd>P</kbd>, then <code>Create: New Jupyter Notebook</code>). Click <strong>Select Kernel</strong> at the top right, pick <code>pandas-quest</code>, run this in a cell, and paste the path it prints.</p></div><div class="q-answer is-paste"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s0p2">paste output</label><textarea id="in-s0p2" rows="6" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" placeholder="/.../envs/pandas-quest/bin/python"></textarea><button type="button" class="q-check">Check output</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p>Not seeing pandas-quest in the kernel list? Click the refresh icon in the kernel picker, or restart VS Code once. The environment needs <code>ipykernel</code> installed, which step 3 did.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one way to get it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">sys</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">sys</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">executable</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div></details></div></div></section>
<section class="q-stage" id="stage-6" data-stage="6"><header class="q-stage-head"><div class="q-stage-title"><span class="q-stage-num">Stage 12<span class="q-badge is-optional">Level up</span></span><h2>Project Lodge: a tidy home and a trustworthy download</h2></div><div class="q-stage-stars" data-stars-for="6" aria-label="Stars earned in this stage"><span class="q-s" data-part="1">&#9733;</span><span class="q-s" data-part="2">&#9733;</span></div></header><p class="q-promise">You will lay out a project folder, download real data with a script, prove the file is intact, and move your function into a module any notebook can import. <span class="q-time">About 50 minutes.</span></p><div class="q-prose"><p>In Part 1 you worked in Colab notebooks, where files live in a temporary session. Real projects live in folders on a disk, so now you will give yours a proper home.</p><p>Analyses that survive are organised. Not fancy, just predictable. When someone (including you in six months) opens the folder, they should know where the raw data is, what is code, and what is output.</p><pre class="q-tree"><code>pandas-quest/
├── README.md              what this is and how to run it
├── environment.yml        the exact recipe for the conda environment
├── data/
│   ├── raw/               downloads exactly as received. Never edited.
│   └── derived/           tables your code produces
├── notebooks/             exploration, in numbered order
├── src/                   functions you reuse (plain .py files)
└── results/
    └── figures/           plots saved by code, not screenshots</code></pre><aside class="q-callout is-why"><strong class="q-callout-title">Two rules that prevent most disasters</strong><ul><li><strong>Raw data is read-only.</strong> Never open a raw file in Excel and save it; Excel silently rewrites numbers, dates and gene names. Your code reads from <code>data/raw</code> and writes to <code>data/derived</code>.</li><li><strong>Everything in <code>results/</code> can be deleted and regenerated</strong> by running your code. If it cannot, you have a hidden manual step.</li></ul></aside><h3>1. Build the skeleton</h3><p>From your project folder, in the terminal where <code>(pandas-quest)</code> is active (this one-liner works on every operating system):</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">terminal</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code>python<span class="w"> </span>-c<span class="w"> </span><span class="s2">"from pathlib import Path; [Path(p).mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True) for p in ['data/raw', 'data/derived', 'notebooks', 'src', 'results/figures']]"</span></code></pre></div><h3>2. Get the data</h3><p>Three files power the rest of the guide. You can click to download them by hand, but the script below is better because it is a record of where each file came from.</p><div class="q-table-wrap"><table class="q-table"><thead><tr><th>File</th><th>What it is</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><a href="/assets/data/toy_vampseq_bin_counts.csv" download>toy_vampseq_bin_counts.csv</a></td><td>Invented table of read counts, used in the pandas stages (3 to 5).</td></tr><tr><td><a href="/assets/data/pten-variant-abundance.csv" download>pten-variant-abundance.csv</a></td><td>The real table: one row per protein variant, used from Stage 6.</td></tr><tr><td><a href="/assets/data/pten-region-annotations.csv" download>pten-region-annotations.csv</a></td><td>Three named stretches of the sequence, used in the region and plotting stages.</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>Save this as <code>download_data.py</code> in the project root and run <code>python download_data.py</code>.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">download_data.py</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="sd">"""Download the data files into data/raw/. Safe to re-run."""</span>
<span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pathlib</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">Path</span>
<span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">urllib.request</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">urlretrieve</span>

<span class="n">BASE</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="s2">"https://atharva-bhagwat.github.io/assets/data/"</span>
<span class="n">FILES</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">{</span>
    <span class="s2">"toy_vampseq_bin_counts.csv"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="n">BASE</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="s2">"toy_vampseq_bin_counts.csv"</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="s2">"pten-variant-abundance.csv"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="n">BASE</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="s2">"pten-variant-abundance.csv"</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="s2">"pten-region-annotations.csv"</span><span class="p">:</span> <span class="n">BASE</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="s2">"pten-region-annotations.csv"</span><span class="p">,</span>
<span class="p">}</span>

<span class="n">out_dir</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">Path</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"data/raw"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">out_dir</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">mkdir</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">parents</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">True</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">exist_ok</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">True</span><span class="p">)</span>

<span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">name</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">url</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">FILES</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">items</span><span class="p">():</span>
    <span class="n">target</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">out_dir</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="n">name</span>
    <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">target</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">exists</span><span class="p">():</span>
        <span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="sa">f</span><span class="s2">"skip   </span><span class="si">{</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="si">}</span><span class="s2"> (already there)"</span><span class="p">)</span>
        <span class="k">continue</span>
    <span class="n">urlretrieve</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">url</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">target</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="sa">f</span><span class="s2">"saved  </span><span class="si">{</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="si">}</span><span class="s2">  (</span><span class="si">{</span><span class="n">target</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">stat</span><span class="p">()</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">st_size</span><span class="si">:</span><span class="s2">,</span><span class="si">}</span><span class="s2"> bytes)"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><aside class="q-callout is-note"><strong class="q-callout-title">Working directory</strong><p><code>Path("data/raw")</code> is a <em>relative</em> path: it means "from wherever Python was started". That is why you run the script from the project root. When something cannot find a file, print <code>Path.cwd()</code> before anything else. In a VS Code notebook, the working directory is the notebook's own folder, <code>notebooks/</code>.</p></aside><h3>3. Check the file is the file</h3><p>A truncated or edited download looks fine until it quietly ruins your results. A <strong>SHA-256 hash</strong> is a fingerprint of a file's exact bytes; change one character and it changes completely. The guide knows the fingerprint of the original file. Compute yours:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">any cell or script</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">hashlib</span>
<span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pathlib</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">Path</span>

<span class="n">path</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">Path</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"data/raw/pten-variant-abundance.csv"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">hashlib</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sha256</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">path</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">read_bytes</span><span class="p">())</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">hexdigest</span><span class="p">())</span></code></pre></div><h3>4. Move your function into <code>src/</code></h3><p>Create <code>src/vampseq_tools.py</code> and paste in the three functions you wrote in Part 1: <code>add_scores</code> (Stage 5), <code>parse_hgvs</code> (Stage 6) and <code>label_regions</code> (Stage 7). The file looks like this:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">src/vampseq_tools.py</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="sd">"""Reusable helpers for the variant-abundance analysis."""</span>
<span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pandas</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="k">as</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pd</span>

<span class="n">BINS</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"bin1_count"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"bin2_count"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"bin3_count"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"bin4_count"</span><span class="p">]</span>


<span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">add_scores</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">weights</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">4</span><span class="p">),</span> <span class="n">min_reads</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">):</span>
<span class="w">    </span><span class="sd">"""Add total_reads and a weighted-average bin score.</span>

<span class="sd">    score = sum(weight_i * count_i) / total_reads. Rows with fewer than</span>
<span class="sd">    `min_reads` total reads get score = NaN. Returns a new DataFrame.</span>
<span class="sd">    """</span>
    <span class="n">out</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">copy</span><span class="p">()</span>
    <span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"total_reads"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">BINS</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">axis</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">(</span><span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">BINS</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">*</span> <span class="nb">list</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">weights</span><span class="p">))</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sum</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">axis</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"total_reads"</span><span class="p">]</span>
    <span class="n">out</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">loc</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"total_reads"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">&lt;</span> <span class="n">min_reads</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="nb">float</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"nan"</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">out</span>


<span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">parse_hgvs</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">column</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"hgvs_pro"</span><span class="p">):</span>
<span class="w">    </span><span class="sd">"""Split names like 'p.Asn12Ile' into ref, position and alt columns.</span>

<span class="sd">    ref = 'Asn', position = 12 (nullable integer), alt = 'Ile'.</span>
<span class="sd">    Synonymous names like 'p.Asn12=' get alt = '='. Anything that does not</span>
<span class="sd">    match (for example the wild-type row '_wt') gets missing values.</span>
<span class="sd">    """</span>
    <span class="n">pattern</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="sa">r</span><span class="s2">"^p\.(?P&lt;ref&gt;[A-Za-z]</span><span class="si">{3}</span><span class="s2">)(?P&lt;position&gt;\d+)(?P&lt;alt&gt;[A-Za-z]</span><span class="si">{3}</span><span class="s2">|=)$"</span>
    <span class="n">parts</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">column</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">str</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">extract</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">pattern</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="n">out</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">copy</span><span class="p">()</span>
    <span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"ref"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">parts</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"ref"</span><span class="p">]</span>
    <span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"position"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">to_numeric</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">parts</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"position"</span><span class="p">])</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">astype</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"Int64"</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"alt"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">parts</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"alt"</span><span class="p">]</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">out</span>


<span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">label_regions</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">regions</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">position_column</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"position"</span><span class="p">):</span>
<span class="w">    </span><span class="sd">"""Add a 'region' column from a table with region, start and end columns.</span>

<span class="sd">    Positions outside every region are labelled 'other'; rows without a</span>
<span class="sd">    position are labelled 'no_position'.</span>
<span class="sd">    """</span>
    <span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">find</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">position</span><span class="p">):</span>
        <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">isna</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">position</span><span class="p">):</span>
            <span class="k">return</span> <span class="s2">"no_position"</span>
        <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">r</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">regions</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">itertuples</span><span class="p">():</span>
            <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">r</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">start</span> <span class="o">&lt;=</span> <span class="n">position</span> <span class="o">&lt;=</span> <span class="n">r</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">end</span><span class="p">:</span>
                <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">r</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">region</span>
        <span class="k">return</span> <span class="s2">"other"</span>

    <span class="n">out</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">copy</span><span class="p">()</span>
    <span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"region"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">out</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">position_column</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">map</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">find</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">out</span></code></pre></div><p>Now any notebook can import it. Put this in the first cell of each notebook:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">first cell of every notebook</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">sys</span>
<span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pathlib</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">Path</span>

<span class="c1"># Make the project's src/ folder importable, wherever the notebook runs from</span>
<span class="n">ROOT</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">Path</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">cwd</span><span class="p">()</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">parent</span> <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">Path</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">cwd</span><span class="p">()</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">name</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"notebooks"</span> <span class="k">else</span> <span class="n">Path</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">cwd</span><span class="p">()</span>
<span class="n">sys</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">path</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">insert</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="nb">str</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">ROOT</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="s2">"src"</span><span class="p">))</span>
<span class="n">RAW</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">ROOT</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="s2">"data"</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="s2">"raw"</span>

<span class="o">%</span><span class="n">load_ext</span> <span class="n">autoreload</span>
<span class="o">%</span><span class="n">autoreload</span> <span class="mi">2</span>

<span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pandas</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="k">as</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pd</span>
<span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">vampseq_tools</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">add_scores</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">parse_hgvs</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">label_regions</span></code></pre></div><ul><li><strong>Why the <code>sys.path</code> line?</strong> Python only imports from certain folders. This adds yours.</li><li><strong>Why <code>%autoreload 2</code>?</strong> The kernel remembers imported code (Stage 1!). Without autoreload, editing <code>vampseq_tools.py</code> changes nothing until you restart the kernel. With it, edits are picked up on the next run.</li></ul><aside class="q-callout is-trap"><strong class="q-callout-title"><code>ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'vampseq_tools'</code></strong><p>Python could not find your file. Is it inside <code>src/</code>? Is the name spelled the same? Did you run the cell with the <code>sys.path</code> line first? Print <code>sys.path[0]</code> and look inside that folder.</p></aside><h3>5. Write it down: README and environment</h3><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">README.md</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="gh"># PTEN variant abundance, a pandas practice project</span>

Explores a published dataset of protein variant abundance scores.

<span class="gu">## Run it</span>
    conda env create -f environment.yml
    conda activate pandas-quest
    python download_data.py
    python src/make_figures.py

<span class="gu">## Layout</span>
data/raw (never edited), data/derived, notebooks/, src/, results/figures/

<span class="gu">## Decisions</span>
<span class="k">-</span><span class="w"> </span>Score = weighted average of the four bins (weights 1-4).
<span class="k">-</span><span class="w"> </span>Variants with fewer than 50 reads are excluded from scores.</code></pre></div><p>Freeze the environment so someone else (or a new laptop) can rebuild it:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">terminal</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code>conda<span class="w"> </span>env<span class="w"> </span><span class="nb">export</span><span class="w"> </span>--from-history<span class="w"> </span>&gt;<span class="w"> </span>environment.yml</code></pre></div><p><code>--from-history</code> records only the packages you asked for, not every dependency, so the file stays portable between operating systems. To rebuild: <code>conda env create -f environment.yml</code>.</p><h3>6. Optional but wise: version control</h3><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">terminal</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code>git<span class="w"> </span>init
<span class="nb">printf</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="s2">"data/raw/\n__pycache__/\n.ipynb_checkpoints/\n.DS_Store\n"</span><span class="w"> </span>&gt;<span class="w"> </span>.gitignore
git<span class="w"> </span>add<span class="w"> </span>.
git<span class="w"> </span>commit<span class="w"> </span>-m<span class="w"> </span><span class="s2">"Project skeleton"</span></code></pre></div><p>Commit whenever something works. A commit is a save point you can always return to, and the message is a one-line diary entry. (On Windows, create <code>.gitignore</code> in VS Code and paste those four lines instead.)</p><h3>7. Check your skeleton</h3><p>Save this as <code>check_project.py</code> in the project root, and run it with <code>python check_project.py</code>:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check_project.py</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pathlib</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">Path</span>

<span class="n">expected</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span>
    <span class="s2">"data/raw"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"data/derived"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"notebooks"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"src"</span><span class="p">,</span>
    <span class="s2">"results/figures"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"README.md"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"environment.yml"</span><span class="p">,</span>
<span class="p">]</span>

<span class="n">found</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="mi">0</span>
<span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">item</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">expected</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="n">ok</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">Path</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">item</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">exists</span><span class="p">()</span>
    <span class="n">found</span> <span class="o">+=</span> <span class="n">ok</span>
    <span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">((</span><span class="s2">"[x] "</span> <span class="k">if</span> <span class="n">ok</span> <span class="k">else</span> <span class="s2">"[ ] "</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">+</span> <span class="n">item</span><span class="p">)</span>

<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="sa">f</span><span class="s2">"project check: </span><span class="si">{</span><span class="n">found</span><span class="si">}</span><span class="s2">/</span><span class="si">{</span><span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">expected</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="si">}</span><span class="s2">"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div></div><div class="q-practice"><h3>Practice: your turn</h3><p>Reading code teaches you what code looks like; writing it teaches you to code. Do these in your notebook. Each has a starter, a <strong>check cell</strong> that stays silent when you are right and raises an error when you are not (read it: that is your Stage 2 skill at work), and a hidden solution. Tick <em>Done</em> when the check prints <code>all checks passed</code>.</p><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e6a"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: file_fingerprint <span class="q-badge is-stretch">Stretch</span></h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Write <code>file_fingerprint(path)</code> that returns the SHA-256 of a file as a hex string. Then add it to <code>src/vampseq_tools.py</code> so any notebook can import it. (Test it here first; the check writes a tiny temporary file.)</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">hashlib</span>
<span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pathlib</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">Path</span>


<span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">file_fingerprint</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">path</span><span class="p">):</span>
    <span class="k">pass</span>   <span class="c1"># TODO</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">tempfile</span>
<span class="k">with</span> <span class="n">tempfile</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">TemporaryDirectory</span><span class="p">()</span> <span class="k">as</span> <span class="n">folder</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="n">p</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">Path</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">folder</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="s2">"abc.txt"</span>
    <span class="n">p</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">write_bytes</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="sa">b</span><span class="s2">"abc"</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">file_fingerprint</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">p</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"ba7816bf8f01cfea414140de5dae2223b00361a396177a9cb410ff61f20015ad"</span>
    <span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">file_fingerprint</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="nb">str</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">p</span><span class="p">))</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="n">file_fingerprint</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">p</span><span class="p">)</span>   <span class="c1"># accepts a str as well</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">hashlib</span>
<span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pathlib</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">Path</span>


<span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">file_fingerprint</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">path</span><span class="p">):</span>
<span class="w">    </span><span class="sd">"""SHA-256 of a file, as a hex string."""</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">hashlib</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sha256</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">Path</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">path</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">read_bytes</span><span class="p">())</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">hexdigest</span><span class="p">()</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e6b"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: ensure_dirs <span class="q-badge is-stretch">Stretch</span></h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Write <code>ensure_dirs(root, names)</code> that creates each folder in <code>names</code> under <code>root</code> (including nested ones such as <code>"results/figures"</code>) and returns the list of names it <strong>newly created</strong>. Calling it twice must not fail, and the second call returns an empty list. This is the skeleton command from earlier, turned into a reusable tool.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pathlib</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">Path</span>


<span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">ensure_dirs</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">root</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">names</span><span class="p">):</span>
    <span class="k">pass</span>   <span class="c1"># TODO</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">tempfile</span>
<span class="k">with</span> <span class="n">tempfile</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">TemporaryDirectory</span><span class="p">()</span> <span class="k">as</span> <span class="n">folder</span><span class="p">:</span>
    <span class="n">wanted</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"data/raw"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"notebooks"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"results/figures"</span><span class="p">]</span>
    <span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">ensure_dirs</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">folder</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">wanted</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="n">wanted</span>
    <span class="k">assert</span> <span class="nb">all</span><span class="p">((</span><span class="n">Path</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">folder</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="n">n</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">is_dir</span><span class="p">()</span> <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">n</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">wanted</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">ensure_dirs</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">folder</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">wanted</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="p">[]</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pathlib</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">Path</span>


<span class="k">def</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nf">ensure_dirs</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">root</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">names</span><span class="p">):</span>
<span class="w">    </span><span class="sd">"""Create folders under root. Return the names that did not exist yet."""</span>
    <span class="n">created</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[]</span>
    <span class="k">for</span> <span class="n">name</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">names</span><span class="p">:</span>
        <span class="n">path</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">Path</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">root</span><span class="p">)</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="n">name</span>
        <span class="k">if</span> <span class="ow">not</span> <span class="n">path</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">exists</span><span class="p">():</span>
            <span class="n">path</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">mkdir</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">parents</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">True</span><span class="p">)</span>
            <span class="n">created</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">append</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">name</span><span class="p">)</span>
    <span class="k">return</span> <span class="n">created</span></code></pre></div></details></div></div><div class="q-puzzles"><div class="q-puzzle" data-qid="s6p1" data-part="1"><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>First star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>Compute the SHA-256 of <code>data/raw/pten-variant-abundance.csv</code> and paste the 64 characters. The checker compares it with the fingerprint of the original file. If it does not match, the file is not the one published, so download again before going further.</p></div><div class="q-answer is-paste"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s6p1">paste output</label><textarea id="in-s6p1" rows="6" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" placeholder="64 hexadecimal characters"></textarea><button type="button" class="q-check">Check output</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p>A mismatch usually means a partial download (check the file size: it should be roughly a megabyte), or the file was opened and re-saved in a spreadsheet program. Delete it and run <code>download_data.py</code> again.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one way to get it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">hashlib</span>
<span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pathlib</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">Path</span>

<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">hashlib</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">sha256</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">Path</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"data/raw/pten-variant-abundance.csv"</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">read_bytes</span><span class="p">())</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">hexdigest</span><span class="p">())</span></code></pre></div></details></div><div class="q-puzzle is-part2" data-qid="s6p2" data-part="2" hidden><div class="q-puzzle-head"><span class="q-star" aria-hidden="true">&#9733;</span><h4>Second star</h4></div><div class="q-puzzle-body"><p>Paste the output of <code>check_project.py</code>. It should end with <code>project check: 7/7</code>.</p></div><div class="q-answer is-paste"><label class="q-prompt" for="in-s6p2">paste output</label><textarea id="in-s6p2" rows="6" spellcheck="false" autocomplete="off" placeholder="[x] data/raw
[x] data/derived
...
project check: 7/7"></textarea><button type="button" class="q-check">Check output</button></div><p class="q-feedback" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p><details class="q-details q-help"><summary>Stuck? Nudge and solution</summary><p>Any line starting with <code>[ ]</code> is missing. Create it in the VS Code Explorer (right-click, New Folder or New File), or re-run the skeleton command.</p></details></div></div></section>
<section class="q-stage" id="stage-13" data-stage="13"><header class="q-stage-head"><div class="q-stage-title"><span class="q-stage-num">Stage 13<span class="q-badge is-optional">Level up</span></span><h2>Ship It: from notebook to script</h2></div></header><p class="q-promise">Make your analysis re-runnable end to end, so that someone else (or you in a year) can rebuild every figure with one command. <span class="q-time">About 40 minutes.</span></p><div class="q-prose"><p>In Part 1 you explored in notebooks. Final results should not depend on cells you clicked in some order. Here is the checklist that turns exploration into something reproducible.</p><ol><li><strong>Restart and Run All</strong> every notebook. Fix anything that fails or depends on a cell you ran once and forgot.</li><li>Move final plotting code into a script so figures regenerate with one command. Save this as <code>src/make_figures.py</code>:</li></ol><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">src/make_figures.py</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="sd">"""Rebuild every figure from the raw data.</span>

<span class="sd">Run from anywhere:  python src/make_figures.py</span>
<span class="sd">"""</span>
<span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pathlib</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">Path</span>

<span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">matplotlib</span>
<span class="n">matplotlib</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">use</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"Agg"</span><span class="p">)</span>                      <span class="c1"># draw straight to files, no window</span>
<span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">matplotlib.pyplot</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="k">as</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">plt</span>
<span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pandas</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="k">as</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pd</span>
<span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">seaborn</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="k">as</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">sns</span>

<span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">vampseq_tools</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">label_regions</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">parse_hgvs</span>   <span class="c1"># same folder as this script</span>

<span class="n">ROOT</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">Path</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="vm">__file__</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">resolve</span><span class="p">()</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">parent</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">parent</span>
<span class="n">RAW</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">FIGS</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">ROOT</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="s2">"data"</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="s2">"raw"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ROOT</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="s2">"results"</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="s2">"figures"</span>
<span class="n">FIGS</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">mkdir</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">parents</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">True</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">exist_ok</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">True</span><span class="p">)</span>

<span class="n">sns</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">set_theme</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">style</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"whitegrid"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">context</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"notebook"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">palette</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"colorblind"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">TYPES</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"synonymous"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"missense"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="s2">"nonsense"</span><span class="p">]</span>
