<span class="n">df</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">read_csv</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">RAW</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="s2">"pten-variant-abundance.csv"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">regions</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">read_csv</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">RAW</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="s2">"pten-region-annotations.csv"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">df</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">label_regions</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">parse_hgvs</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">),</span> <span class="n">regions</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">data</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"record_type"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">isin</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">TYPES</span><span class="p">)]</span>

<span class="n">fig</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">axes</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">plt</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">subplots</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">2</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">figsize</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">10</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="mi">4</span><span class="p">))</span>
<span class="n">sns</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">ecdfplot</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">data</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">data</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">x</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">hue</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"record_type"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">hue_order</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">TYPES</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ax</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">axes</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">])</span>
<span class="n">sns</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">boxplot</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">data</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">data</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">x</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"record_type"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">y</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">order</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">TYPES</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ax</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="n">axes</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">])</span>
<span class="n">axes</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">set</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">xlabel</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"abundance score"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ylabel</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"cumulative fraction"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">axes</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="mi">1</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">set</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">xlabel</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">""</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">ylabel</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"abundance score"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">fig</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">tight_layout</span><span class="p">()</span>
<span class="n">fig</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">savefig</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">FIGS</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="s2">"score_distributions.png"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">dpi</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="mi">300</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">bbox_inches</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="s2">"tight"</span><span class="p">)</span>

<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"wrote"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">FIGS</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="s2">"score_distributions.png"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><ol><li>Run it from the project root: <code>python src/make_figures.py</code>. Delete <code>results/figures</code> first to prove it rebuilds everything.</li><li>Re-export the environment: <code>conda env export --from-history &gt; environment.yml</code>.</li><li>Update the README with the dial you chose and why, then commit.</li></ol><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">terminal</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code>python<span class="w"> </span>src/make_figures.py</code></pre></div><p>Your final <code>src/vampseq_tools.py</code> now holds <code>add_scores</code>, <code>parse_hgvs</code> and <code>label_regions</code>: three small functions, each documented, each reused by notebooks and scripts alike. That is the whole habit in miniature.</p><h3>Where to go next</h3><ul><li><a href="https://pandas.pydata.org/docs/user_guide/10min.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener">10 minutes to pandas</a>: the official short tour. You can now read all of it.</li><li><a href="https://seaborn.pydata.org/examples/index.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener">seaborn gallery</a> and <a href="https://matplotlib.org/stable/gallery/index.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener">matplotlib gallery</a>: browse for a plot shape, copy the code, adapt it.</li><li>Put your project on GitHub so your future lab can see it.</li><li>Pick a question of your own about this dataset and answer it with a figure, a paragraph, and a function.</li></ul></div><div class="q-practice"><h3>Practice: your turn</h3><p>Reading code teaches you what code looks like; writing it teaches you to code. Do these in your notebook. Each has a starter, a <strong>check cell</strong> that stays silent when you are right and raises an error when you are not (read it: that is your Stage 2 skill at work), and a hidden solution. Tick <em>Done</em> when the check prints <code>all checks passed</code>.</p><div class="q-ex" data-ex="e11b"><div class="q-ex-head"><h4>Your turn: your own report script <span class="q-badge is-stretch">Stretch</span></h4><label class="q-ex-done"><input type="checkbox" class="q-ex-check"> Done</label></div><div class="q-ex-task">Create <code>src/report.py</code>: a script, not a notebook, that loads the two CSVs from <code>data/raw</code> (build the paths from <code>Path(__file__)</code> so it works from anywhere), parses and labels regions with your module, then prints exactly: a line <code>rows: N</code>, a line <code>missense: N</code>, and the mean missense score per region (rounded to 3 places). Run it from the terminal with <code>python src/report.py</code>. The check cell below runs it for you.</div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">starter: copy into a cell and complete it</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="sd">"""Print a short report about the variant table."""</span>
<span class="c1"># TODO: imports, paths from __file__, load, parse, label, print three things</span></code></pre></div><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">check: run this after your code</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">subprocess</span><span class="o">,</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">sys</span>
<span class="n">out</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">subprocess</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">run</span><span class="p">([</span><span class="n">sys</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">executable</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="nb">str</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">ROOT</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="s2">"src"</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="s2">"report.py"</span><span class="p">)],</span> <span class="n">capture_output</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">True</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">text</span><span class="o">=</span><span class="kc">True</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="n">out</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">returncode</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="mi">0</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">out</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">stderr</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="s2">"rows:"</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">out</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">stdout</span> <span class="ow">and</span> <span class="s2">"missense:"</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">out</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">stdout</span>
<span class="k">assert</span> <span class="s2">"phosphatase_domain"</span> <span class="ow">in</span> <span class="n">out</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">stdout</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"all checks passed"</span><span class="p">)</span></code></pre></div><details class="q-details q-ex-sol"><summary>Show a solution (try for ten minutes first)</summary><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">one solution</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pathlib</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">Path</span>

<span class="kn">import</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pandas</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="k">as</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">pd</span>

<span class="kn">from</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="nn">vampseq_tools</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="kn">import</span> <span class="n">label_regions</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="n">parse_hgvs</span>

<span class="n">ROOT</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">Path</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="vm">__file__</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">resolve</span><span class="p">()</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">parent</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">parent</span>
<span class="n">df</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">read_csv</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">ROOT</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="s2">"data"</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="s2">"raw"</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="s2">"pten-variant-abundance.csv"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">regions</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">pd</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">read_csv</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">ROOT</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="s2">"data"</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="s2">"raw"</span> <span class="o">/</span> <span class="s2">"pten-region-annotations.csv"</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">df</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">label_regions</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">parse_hgvs</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">),</span> <span class="n">regions</span><span class="p">)</span>
<span class="n">miss</span> <span class="o">=</span> <span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">[</span><span class="s2">"record_type"</span><span class="p">]</span> <span class="o">==</span> <span class="s2">"missense"</span><span class="p">]</span>

<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"rows:"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">df</span><span class="p">))</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"missense:"</span><span class="p">,</span> <span class="nb">len</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">miss</span><span class="p">))</span>
<span class="nb">print</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="n">miss</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">groupby</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="s2">"region"</span><span class="p">)[</span><span class="s2">"score"</span><span class="p">]</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">mean</span><span class="p">()</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">round</span><span class="p">(</span><span class="mi">3</span><span class="p">)</span><span class="o">.</span><span class="n">to_string</span><span class="p">())</span></code></pre></div></details></div></div><div class="q-puzzles"></div></section>
<section class="q-stage" id="hpc"><header class="q-stage-head"><div class="q-stage-title"><span class="q-stage-num">After the summit</span><h2>Taking your work to the HPC cluster</h2></div></header><p class="q-promise">What this guide was for, when a laptop stops being enough, and how to move the same project onto the cluster. <span class="q-time">No stars here, just a map.</span></p><div class="q-prose"><h3>What this guide was really for</h3><p>Your laptop is where you learn and where you develop. Data is small, feedback is instant, and a mistake costs seconds. Everything in the twelve stages was practice for the habits that make a cluster usable later:</p><div class="q-table-wrap"><table class="q-table"><thead><tr><th>Habit you built</th><th>Why the cluster needs it</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Isolated environment plus <code>environment.yml</code></td><td>You rebuild the exact same Python on the cluster in one command.</td></tr><tr><td>Scripts like <code>src/make_figures.py</code></td><td>A cluster job runs a script, not a notebook you click through.</td></tr><tr><td>Relative paths from a project root</td><td>The same code runs in a different folder on a different machine.</td></tr><tr><td>Functions with parameters</td><td>One script can process any sample or setting you hand it.</td></tr><tr><td>Raw data never edited, results regenerated</td><td>Jobs can be re-run safely, and you always know where an output came from.</td></tr><tr><td>Reading the last line of an error</td><td>Failed jobs leave you a log file full of tracebacks. You already know how to read one.</td></tr></tbody></table></div><h3>When do you actually need HPC?</h3><p>Not for this dataset: four thousand rows fit on any laptop. Develop small, then scale up when one of these happens:</p><ul><li><strong>Memory:</strong> you hit <code>MemoryError</code>, or your laptop starts crawling because the data does not fit in RAM.</li><li><strong>Time:</strong> one run takes hours, or you need to repeat it for many samples or many parameter settings.</li><li><strong>Hardware:</strong> you need many cores at once, or a GPU.</li></ul><aside class="q-callout is-trap"><strong class="q-callout-title">Never run heavy work on a login node</strong><p>When you log in, you land on a shared machine used by everyone to edit files and submit jobs. Anything big belongs in a job that the scheduler (Slurm) places on a compute node. Start with an interactive job to try things, and a batch job once it works.</p></aside><h3>The same project, on the cluster</h3><ol><li><strong>Get access.</strong> Request an account and read how resources are allocated: <a href="https://crc-pages.pitt.edu/user-manual/getting-started/getting-started-step1-account/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">getting an account</a> and <a href="https://crc-pages.pitt.edu/user-manual/keystone/requesting/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">requesting resources</a>.</li><li><strong>Log in.</strong> Use a terminal (<a href="https://crc-pages.pitt.edu/user-manual/getting-started/terminal/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">SSH</a>) or the browser portal (<a href="https://crc-pages.pitt.edu/user-manual/getting-started/open-ondemand/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Open OnDemand</a>). OnDemand also offers <a href="https://crc-pages.pitt.edu/user-manual/getting-started/quickstart-python-ondemand/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Python and Jupyter sessions</a> if you want a notebook on a compute node.</li><li><strong>Bring your code.</strong> Put the project in a Git repository on your laptop, then <code>git clone</code> it on the cluster. Code travels through Git; it keeps a history and avoids "which copy is newest?".</li><li><strong>Bring your data.</strong> Large files go through a transfer tool, not Git. See <a href="https://crc-pages.pitt.edu/user-manual/data-management/file-transfer-methods/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">file transfer</a> (<a href="https://crc-pages.pitt.edu/user-manual/data-management/file-transfer-methods/globus/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Globus</a> is the usual choice) and the <a href="https://crc-pages.pitt.edu/user-manual/data-management/file-systems/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">file systems</a> page to decide where files should live. Big temporary files belong in <a href="https://crc-pages.pitt.edu/user-manual/slurm/scratch-storage/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">scratch</a>.</li><li><strong>Rebuild the environment.</strong> Load conda or Python the way the <a href="https://crc-pages.pitt.edu/user-manual/applications/python/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Python page</a> describes, then recreate yours: <code>conda env create -f environment.yml</code>. This is why you froze it in Stage 12.</li><li><strong>Test on a small slice.</strong> Ask for a short <a href="https://crc-pages.pitt.edu/user-manual/slurm/interactive-jobs/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">interactive job</a>, run your script on a few rows, and fix errors where you can see them.</li><li><strong>Submit a batch job.</strong> Write a short job script and hand it to Slurm (<a href="https://crc-pages.pitt.edu/user-manual/slurm/batch-jobs/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">batch jobs</a>).</li><li><strong>Check, then copy results home.</strong> Read the log, confirm the outputs look right, and copy <code>results/</code> back to your laptop for figures and writing.</li></ol><h3>A job script</h3><p>A batch script is a shell script with <code>#SBATCH</code> lines that tell Slurm what to reserve. The names of clusters and partitions, and sensible memory and time requests, are specific to your group; copy real values from the manual rather than from this sketch.</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">run_make_figures.sh</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code><span class="ch">#!/bin/bash</span>
<span class="c1">#SBATCH --job-name=make-figures</span>
<span class="c1">#SBATCH --cluster=CLUSTER_NAME        # placeholder: copy real values from the manual</span>
<span class="c1">#SBATCH --partition=PARTITION_NAME    # placeholder</span>
<span class="c1">#SBATCH --nodes=1</span>
<span class="c1">#SBATCH --cpus-per-task=4</span>
<span class="c1">#SBATCH --mem=16G</span>
<span class="c1">#SBATCH --time=01:00:00</span>
<span class="c1">#SBATCH --output=logs/%x-%j.out       # %x = job name, %j = job id</span>

<span class="c1"># 1. make the same Python the same way you did on your laptop</span>
<span class="c1">#    (how to load conda or Python on the cluster is on the manual's Python page)</span>
conda<span class="w"> </span>activate<span class="w"> </span>pandas-quest

<span class="c1"># 2. run from the project root so relative paths behave</span>
<span class="nb">cd</span><span class="w"> </span><span class="s2">"</span><span class="nv">$SLURM_SUBMIT_DIR</span><span class="s2">"</span>

<span class="c1"># 3. run the script, not a notebook</span>
python<span class="w"> </span>src/make_figures.py</code></pre></div><p>Submit it and keep an eye on it:</p><div class="qcode"><div class="qcode-bar"><span class="qcode-name">terminal on the cluster</span><button class="qcopy" type="button">Copy</button></div><pre><code>mkdir<span class="w"> </span>-p<span class="w"> </span>logs
sbatch<span class="w"> </span>run_make_figures.sh<span class="w">     </span><span class="c1"># prints: Submitted batch job 12345678</span>
squeue<span class="w"> </span>--me<span class="w">                    </span><span class="c1"># what is queued or running</span>
sacct<span class="w"> </span>-j<span class="w"> </span><span class="m">12345678</span><span class="w">              </span><span class="c1"># what happened, after it finishes</span>
scancel<span class="w"> </span><span class="m">12345678</span><span class="w">               </span><span class="c1"># stop it</span></code></pre></div><aside class="q-callout is-note"><strong class="q-callout-title">Reading a failed job</strong><p>The log file in <code>logs/</code> is your traceback. Open it and go to the bottom, exactly like Stage 2. A job killed for time or memory says so: ask for a little more of what ran out, not ten times more. Smaller requests usually start sooner.</p></aside><h3>Habits that make cluster work painless</h3><ul><li><strong>Develop small, run big.</strong> Add a small slice or a <code>--sample</code> option to your script so you can test in seconds.</li><li><strong>Many similar tasks?</strong> Do not write a loop in one long job; use a <a href="https://crc-pages.pitt.edu/user-manual/slurm/job-arrays/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">job array</a> so each sample runs on its own.</li><li><strong>Keep sessions alive.</strong> <a href="https://crc-pages.pitt.edu/user-manual/tips/persist-sessions/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">tmux and nohup</a> let long-running work survive a dropped connection.</li><li><strong>Edit comfortably.</strong> VS Code can work on cluster files directly: see the <a href="https://crc-pages.pitt.edu/user-manual/slurm/vscode/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">VS Code page</a>.</li><li><strong>Record what you ran.</strong> Commit the job script, the <code>environment.yml</code> and the README. Next month, they are your memory.</li><li><strong>Stuck?</strong> Check <a href="https://crc-pages.pitt.edu/user-manual/faq/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Common Problems</a> and the <a href="https://crc-pages.pitt.edu/user-manual/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">main user manual</a>, then ask the support team with the job ID and the last lines of the log.</li></ul><p>The full documentation for everything above lives in the <a href="https://crc-pages.pitt.edu/user-manual/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">CRCD User Manual</a>.</p></div></section>

Sign the register: enter your name, then print it or save it as a PDF.</p><div class="q-cert-controls"><input id="q-cert-input" type="text" placeholder="Your name" autocomplete="name" aria-label="Your name"><button type="button" class="q-check" id="q-cert-print">Print certificate</button></div><div class="q-cert"><p>This certifies that</p><p class="q-cert-name" id="q-cert-name">Your name here</p><p>completed <strong>Introduction to Bioinformatics Programming in Python</strong>: set up a Python environment, learned pandas, wrote reusable functions, audited a real dataset and plotted it more than a dozen ways.</p><p class="q-cert-stars" id="q-cert-stars" aria-hidden="true"></p><p class="q-cert-rank" id="q-cert-rank"></p><p><small>atharvabhagwat.dev</small></p></div></section>
